```yaml
  end_char: 31917
  text_sha256: 7623595c68f3de161a5f1e8184020c8ef0255b37fd3e33843b0be27698992401
greek_terms:
  - μοίρας
  - διένειμεν
  - διπλασίαν
  - τριπλασίαν
  - ἑπτακαιεικοσιπλασίαν
english_gloss: ""
observation: "After mixing the soul-substance from being, sameness, and difference into a unity, the demiurge divides the whole into portions (moiras) according to a numerical ratio sequence: 1, 2, 3, 4, 8, 9, 27 (35b)."
textual_basis: The text states that the demiurge made one from three and divided the whole into as many portions as was fitting, each portion itself mixed from Same, Different, and Being. The division then enumerates the sequence by doubles and triples through the seventh portion, including the eightfold and twenty-sevenfold portions.
limits: This records the mathematical structure of the division. It does not interpret the ratios' musical or astronomical significance, which the passage may develop later.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0094
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 38a-38b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 38a-38b
  start_marker: 38a
  end_marker: 38b
  start_char: 35973
  end_char: 36847
  text_sha256: bea9e5183210b4a11c84c74628950850362f6bf756ec4bfd7bebe7057c60a4d1
greek_terms:
  - ἦν
  - ἔσται
  - χρόνος
english_gloss: "'was' and 'will be'; eternity vs. time"
observation: |
  At 38a, Timaeus asserts that the temporal predicates 'was' and 'will be' apply only to becoming (genesis) in time, while what is always in the same state without motion (to aei kata tauta echon akinetos) cannot properly be said to become older or younger, to have become, to have come to be now, or to be going to be in the future.
textual_basis: |
  The passage explicitly distinguishes two registers: 'was' and 'will be' are called movements (kineseis) proper to genesis in time, while the eternal unchanging being receives none of the temporal determinations that genesis attaches to things carried in perception. The contrast between eternal being and time-bound becoming is drawn through the denial of temporal predicates to the former.
limits: |
  The passage does not use the term 'form' (eidos/idea) in this denial, nor does it specify participation or separation vocabulary. The observation records a predicate denial pattern that is structurally akin to form-language claims about eternality but does not prove Timaeus is speaking about Platonic forms per se.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0095
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 38a-38c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 38a-38c
  start_marker: 38a
  end_marker: 38c
  start_char: 35973
  end_char: 37292
  text_sha256: 3e44f7a15ca312496be40e93e51a2bdcf177739d12b3576aa4c588c94f62c201
greek_terms:
  - χρόνος
  - μιμουμένου
  - κυκλουμένου
  - παράδειγμα
  - ὁμοιότατος
english_gloss: time as moving image of eternity; mimetic relation
observation: |
  Timaeus describes time as having come into being as a moving image of eternity (aionos eikona), remaining according to number (kat' arithmon) while circling. This mimetic relationship between time and eternity is stated at 38a, where time imitates eternity and circles according to number, and again at 38b where time comes into being together with the heaven so that they might be as similar as possible to the eternal paradigm.
textual_basis: |
  At 38a, time is said to imitate eternity and circle according to number. At 38b-38c, time comes into being with the heaven according to the paradigm of everlasting nature so that it may be as similar as possible to it.
limits: |
  This observation records the mimetic relation of time to eternity stated in the text. It does not evaluate whether this image-of-eternity claim is consistent with other Platonic treatments of time, nor does it claim the passage is a form-participation claim rather than a cosmological image claim.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0096
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 38a-38b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 38a-38b
  start_marker: 38a
  end_marker: 38b
  start_char: 35973
  end_char: 36847
  text_sha256: bea9e5183210b4a11c84c74628950850362f6bf756ec4bfd7bebe7057c60a4d1
greek_terms:
  - διακριβολογεῖσθαι
  - καιρὸς
english_gloss: to speak precisely; appropriate occasion
observation: |
  At 38b, after listing the temporal distinctions of 'what has come to be', 'what is coming to be', 'what is going to come to be', and 'what is not being', Timaeus remarks that none of these are spoken with precision (akribes), and that the present moment may not be the fitting occasion (kairos) to speak precisely (diakribologeisthai) about them. He then moves on without pursuing the precise account.
textual_basis: |
  Timaeus says 'none of which we speak precisely' (hon ouden akribes legomen), then adds 'about these matters it might not be a fitting occasion in the present to speak with precision' (peri men oun touton tach' an ouk eie kairos prepon en to paronti diakribologeisthai). The speaker names his own lack of precision and defers the precise treatment, then continues the discourse.
limits: |
  This is a local deferral move at 38b only. It does not establish that the entire dialogue operates under a systematic precision disclaimer, nor does it identify irony. It records a patterned speaker move where precision is acknowledged as absent and deferred, which structurally resembles irony-marker deferrals elsewhere.
review_status: rejected
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0097

greek_terms:
  - ἥλιον
english_gloss: ""
observation: The god kindles a light (the sun) in the second orbit from earth so that the heavens would be illuminated as completely as possible and living beings would partake of number by learning from the revolution of the Same and the Similar.
textual_basis: "At 39b the text states that the god (ho theos) lit a light in the second of the orbits around earth, which we now call the sun (helion), for two purposes: to shine through the whole heaven (eis hapanta phainoi ton ouranon) and so that living beings (zoa) might share in number (arithmou) by learning from the revolution of the Same and Similar (tes tautou kai homoiou periphoras). This maps the divine craftsman's production (ignition of the sun) onto a target domain of enabling cosmic time-measurement and arithmetical cognition by ensouled beings."
limits: The observation records the craft-like production act and its stated purposes. It does not evaluate the astronomy, analyze the causal mechanism of ignition, or claim that this passage establishes the demiurge-as-craftsman motif on its own.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0098
source_work: Timaeus

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

  end_char: 39185
  text_sha256: 6fa759a1333e8f47f01235f2afa5210e43bdd04f6bf0ab97de4cb57759656aa3
greek_terms:
  - νὺξ
  - ἡμέρα
  - μεὶς
  - ἐνιαυτὸς
  - περίοδος
english_gloss: ""
observation: "Night and day, month, and year are each defined by reference to a celestial period: night-and-day by the single wisest revolution, month by the moon overtaking the sun, and year by the sun completing its own circle."
textual_basis: At 39c, Timaeus gives a compact sequence of temporal definitions. Night and day are the period of the one wisest revolution; month occurs when the moon completes its circle and overtakes the sun; year occurs when the sun completes its own circle.
limits: This records the definitional sequence for temporal units. It does not assess astronomical accuracy, and it does not include the following few-versus-many claim about unnamed planetary periods.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0099
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 39b-39c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 39b-39c
  start_marker: 39b
  end_marker: 39c

  end_char: 39185
  text_sha256: 6fa759a1333e8f47f01235f2afa5210e43bdd04f6bf0ab97de4cb57759656aa3
greek_terms:
  - ὀλίγοι
  - πολλῶν
english_gloss: ""
observation: The speaker contrasts the few (oligoi) who have conceived the periods of the other planets with the many (polloi) who have not, and states that the many neither name them nor measure them by numbers.
textual_basis: At 39c the text says that humans, except a few of the many (plen oligoi ton pollon), have not conceived (ouk ennenoekotes) the periods of the other planets and therefore neither name nor symmetrically measure them by numbers. This draws an explicit cognitive/naming divide between a knowledgeable few and the ignorant many.
limits: The observation records the few-versus-many distinction as a prosopographic feature of the passage. It does not identify who the few are, compare this to other dialogues' few/many contrasts, or claim an esoteric teaching.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0100
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 37b-37c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 37b-37c
  start_marker: 37b
  end_marker: 37c
  start_char: 34360
  end_char: 35228
  text_sha256: 78a233a4efd1b84541609e1ccf2c7af5c751736242cbe6afb5add298a7dd66e0
greek_terms:
  - νοῦς
  - ἐπιστήμη
english_gloss: ""
observation: At 37b-37c the text states that understanding and knowledge are produced when the soul's circle of the Same grasps the intelligible. If anyone claims that the locus where these two arise is anything other than soul, he speaks falsely. The passage thus makes soul the exclusive substrate of understanding and knowledge.
textual_basis: The clause at 37c states that if anyone claims the seat of these two (understanding and knowledge) is anything other than soul, he will speak anything but the truth. "These two" refers to the two cognitive products just enumerated at 37c. The text explicitly bars any identification of their seat with anything other than soul.
limits: This observation does not claim that the passage defines soul or that the text is committed to a specific psychology. It only records the explicit exclusion of any non-soul substrate for understanding and knowledge.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

greek_terms:
  - ἀόρατος
  - λογισμοῦ δὲ μετέχουσα
  - ἁρμονίας
english_gloss: invisible but partaking in reasoning and harmony
observation: The world soul is characterized as invisible, partaking in reasoning and harmony, and is described as the best of generated things, created by the best of intelligible beings.
textual_basis: At 36e-37a, after describing the construction of the world soul's extension, Timaeus states that the soul itself is invisible, partakes in reasoning and harmony, and is generated as the best of generated things by the best of intelligible beings, using the superlatives 'best' (aristou / aristê).
limits: This observation records the explicit characterization of the world soul. It does not address whether this is a form, an intermediary, or a mathematical structure.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0102
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 36e-37a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

  end_char: 34360
  text_sha256: 33bc0f6a1f3a05d85f82f8f54935778bf81e5aa83e0807979e1b33028685b48f
greek_terms:
  - ταὐτοῦ
  - θατέρου
  - οὐσίας
  - συγκραθεῖσα
english_gloss: mixed from the Same, the Other, and Being
observation: "The world soul is composed by mixing three ingredients: the nature of the Same, the nature of the Other, and being. These are described as being mixed from three portions, blended together."
textual_basis: At 37a, the text says the soul is composed out of the nature of the Same and of the Other and out of being, mixed together from these three portions. The Same and Other are core categories in the dialogue's account of intelligible reality.
limits: This observation records the compositional vocabulary. It does not assess whether the mixture implies the soul is a form, a derived entity, or something else. Nor does it address the intermediate-status debate in later Platonism.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0103

greek_terms:
  - ἀνὰ λόγον μερισθεῖσα
  - συνδεθεῖσα
  - ἀνακυκλουμένη
english_gloss: divided proportionally, bound together, and revolving back upon itself
observation: The world soul is proportionally divided, bound together, and revolves back upon itself. These operations echo the craftsman-like language used throughout the Timaeus for the construction of the cosmos.
textual_basis: At 37a, the text states that the soul, having been mixed from the three portions, is divided according to proportion and bound together, and then describes its self-revolving motion. The vocabulary of dividing and binding employs the same demiurgic craft register found throughout the dialogue's cosmogonic account.
limits: This observation registers the craft-like language of proportional division and binding applied to the soul at 37a. It does not claim that the soul is a physical artifact or that the demiurge literally constructs it with tools.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: Timaeus characterizes the immediately preceding detail as a digression (parergon) that would require more work than the main argument warrants, and defers a fuller treatment to a later, leisured occasion.
textual_basis: At the 38e marker Timaeus says that the topic is a parergon and would produce more work than the things for which it is said, then states that these matters will perhaps receive an account worthy of them later, at leisure (kata scholēn hysteron).
limits: This observation records only the procedural deferral; it does not evaluate whether the deferred account is ever given elsewhere in the dialogue.
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0105
source_work: Timaeus

greek_terms:
  - ταὐτοῦ
  - θατέρου
  - ἕλικα
  - πλαγίαν
english_gloss: ""
observation: Timaeus describes the motion of the heavenly bodies as an interaction between the circle of the Same and the oblique circle of the Different, producing an apparent spiral (helix) in which faster bodies seem to be overtaken by slower ones.
textual_basis: At 38e-39a, the text describes bodies bound with living bonds, the motion of the Different as oblique (plagian) passing through the motion of the Same which dominates it, with smaller circles moving faster and larger slower; the motion of the Same turns all their circles into a spiral (helika) so that the fastest appear to catch up with and be caught by the slower.
limits: This records only the kinematic description of planetary motion through the Same/Different framework; it does not assess the astronomical accuracy of the model or whether the passage functions as myth or proto-science.
review_status: rejected
```

```yaml
observation: Timaeus says the cosmos still lacked the full set of living kinds contained in the intelligible model, then enumerates four kinds and describes the divine kind as fashioned mostly from fire.
textual_basis: "At 39e-40a, Timaeus says the remaining deficiency was that the cosmos had not yet included all living things within itself, and that this remainder was fashioned according to the model. He then enumerates four kinds: heavenly gods, winged air-travelers, water-dwellers, and land-walkers, before saying the divine kind was fashioned mostly from fire."
limits: This repairs the source span so the enumeration is complete. It records the fashioning sequence and does not include the later two-motion assignment, which is separately recorded in the next accepted observation.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0107
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 40a-40b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 40a-40b
  start_marker: 40a
  end_marker: 40b
  start_char: 40173
  end_char: 41088
  text_sha256: 10024d0bdaf3d3cfe12e51eed91efbc5989e5ddce834dba9359046a27dc0de57
greek_terms:
  - τὴν μὲν ἐν ταὐτῷ κατὰ ταὐτά
  - τὴν δὲ εἰς τὸ πρόσθεν
  - ὑπὸ τῆς ταὐτοῦ καὶ ὁμοίου περιφορᾶς κρατουμένῳ
  - κινήσεις δὲ δύο προσῆψεν ἑκάστῳ
english_gloss: ""
observation: |
  At 40a-b each divine living body receives two explicit motions: one of sameness revolving uniformly about the same things, and one forward motion, which is dominated by the revolution of the Same and Similar.
textual_basis: |
  The text at 40a-b explicitly states that the demiurge attached two movements to each heavenly body, naming the motion of sameness first and the forward motion second, and describing the forward motion as being mastered by the Same-and-Similar revolution.
limits: |
  The observation does not interpret the cosmological or astronomical implications of the two-motion scheme or its relation to the five other motions mentioned in the same passage. It only records the explicit two-motion assignment.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0108

greek_terms:
  - ὅσ’ ἀπλανῆ τῶν ἄστρων
  - τρεπόμενα καὶ πλάνην τοιαύτην ἴσχοντα
  - κατὰ ταὐτὰ ἐν ταὐτῷ στρεφόμενα ἀεὶ μένει
english_gloss: ""
observation: |
  At 40b the text distinguishes two classes of heavenly bodies: the fixed stars, which are divine, eternal, and always remain rotating in the same place according to sameness, and the wandering stars, which have a different kind of motion and are generated according to a different principle.
textual_basis: |
  The text at 40b explicitly contrasts the two groups: the fixed stars are described as divine living beings, eternal, and unmoving in their sameness-rotation, while the wandering bodies are said to turn and possess wandering motion, with their generation explained by reference to what was said earlier.
limits: |
  The observation does not identify which specific planets or stars belong to each class, nor does it assess the astronomical accuracy of the distinction. It only records the textual contrast between the two kinds of heavenly bodies.
review_status: rejected
```

```yaml
  The earth is described as our nurse, as wound or revolving around the all-stretched pole, and as guardian and maker of night and day.
textual_basis: |
  At 40b-40c the text introduces the earth with the epithet "our nurse," pairs it with the participle for winding or revolving around the pole, and assigns it the roles of guardian and maker of night and day.
limits: |
  The observation does not resolve whether the participle means "winding" (packed round) or "revolving." It does not claim that the earth is an unmoved center or a moving planet, and it does not follow the later account of planetary dances and conjunctions.
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0110
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 41b-41c

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

  end_marker: 41c
  start_char: 42792
  end_char: 43707
  text_sha256: d74d0a29eb3e0a3bc6384cc560b38a04d0b43a760c5287db4acd87e84ae47b02
greek_terms:
  - τῆς ἐμῆς βουλήσεως μείζονος ἔτι δεσμοῦ καὶ κυριωτέρου
  - θνητὰ ἔτι γένη λοιπὰ τρία ἀγέννητα
english_gloss: ""
observation: |
  The Demiurge declares that the generated gods, though not intrinsically immortal or indissoluble, will escape death because his will (boulēsis) constitutes a bond stronger and more authoritative than the bonds with which they were put together at their genesis (41b).
  This is a divine address embedded in a cosmological myth.
textual_basis: |
  At 41b the Demiurge speaks directly to the generated gods, stating they are not athanatoi (immortal) nor alutoi (indissoluble) yet will not be dissolved nor meet death's portion, because they have received a greater and more authoritative bond of his will than the bonds used when they were put together.
limits: |
  The observation does not analyze the metaphysical status of the Demiurge's will or evaluate whether this constitutes a genuine immortality guarantee.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0111
source_work: Timaeus

greek_terms:
  - τρέπεσθε κατὰ φύσιν ὑμεῖς ἐπὶ τὴν τῶν ζῴων δημιουργίαν
  - μιμούμενοι τὴν ἐμὴν δύναμιν
english_gloss: ""
observation: |
  The Demiurge delegates the production of three remaining mortal kinds to the generated gods, instructing them to turn to the creation of living beings by imitating his own power as demonstrated in their own genesis (41b-c).
textual_basis: |
  At 41c the Demiurge says there remain three ungenerated mortal kinds, and if they are not produced the heaven will be incomplete. He then commands the gods: "turn according to nature to the demiurgy of living beings, imitating my power concerning your own genesis." This frames the Demiurge as a master craftsman delegating work to apprentice craftsmen.
limits: |
  The observation does not address whether the delegation implies a limitation on the Demiurge's own creative capacity or whether it is a dramatic necessity of the myth.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0112
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 41b-41c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 41b-41c
  start_marker: 41b
  end_marker: 41c
  start_char: 42792
  end_char: 43707
  text_sha256: d74d0a29eb3e0a3bc6384cc560b38a04d0b43a760c5287db4acd87e84ae47b02
greek_terms:
  - οὐρανὸς ἀτελὴς ἔσται
  - τέλεος ἱκανῶς
  - θνητὰ ἔτι γένη λοιπὰ τρία ἀγέννητα
english_gloss: ""
observation: |
  The Demiurge states a teleological requirement for cosmic completion: three mortal kinds remain ungenerated, and without their generation the heaven will be incomplete (atelēs). The heaven must contain all kinds of living beings if it is to be sufficiently complete (teleos hikanōs) (41b-c).
textual_basis: |
  At 41b-c the Demiurge says three mortal kinds remain ungenerated; if these do not come into being, "the heaven will be incomplete" (ouranos atelēs estai), and "it must be, if it is to be sufficiently complete" (dei de, ei mellei teleos hikanōs einai). The cosmos must contain all genera of living things.
limits: |
  The observation does not identify what the three mortal kinds are or evaluate whether the teleological argument succeeds as a philosophical claim.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0113
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 39d-39e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 39d-39e
  start_marker: 39d
  end_marker: 39e
  start_char: 39185
  end_char: 40173
  text_sha256: 70746fb17e6505f8c7e6bcb6abfeb778bb6378c5724a2eacd5d56b1e50cfcf23
greek_terms:
  - νοητῷ ζῴῳ
  - παραδείγματος
  - διαιωνίας μίμησιν φύσεως
english_gloss: ""
observation: The cosmos is generated so as to be as similar as possible to the perfect and intelligible Living Creature (39d), imitating its everlasting nature. The paradigm is explicitly named as the model that the craftsman copies in completing the cosmos (39e).
textual_basis: At 39d-39e, the text states the stars were generated "so that this cosmos would be as similar as possible to the perfect and intelligible Living Creature for the imitation of its everlasting nature," and that the craftsman crafted the remainder "fashioning it in imitation of the nature of the paradigm."
limits: This does not prove that the paradigm is a separately existing Form; it records that paradigm-language serves as a standard for cosmic production within the Timaeus cosmology.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0114
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 39d-39e

greek_terms:
  - ἀπηργάζετο
  - ἀποτυπούμενος
english_gloss: ""
observation: The demiurge completes the cosmic production by crafting the still-incomplete cosmos in imitation of the paradigm's nature (39e). The production vocabulary maps the demiurge's activity as a craftsman who fashions his work by looking to a model.
textual_basis: At 39e, the text states that the remaining part "he was crafting, fashioning it in imitation of the nature of the paradigm." The verbs of production and the paradigm-as-model structure are characteristic of the demiurge-as-craftsman mapping.
limits: This observation records the crafting vocabulary and paradigm-model structure. It does not establish the metaphysical status of the paradigm or the demiurge, nor does it claim this is the only or primary mode of cosmic production in the dialogue.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0115
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 42c-42d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 42c-42d
  start_marker: 42c
  end_marker: 42d
  start_char: 45132
  end_char: 45878
  text_sha256: 255991bc3b701225faca13584d8ca922b91163bd17d32e10151623b5140d11db
greek_terms:
  - δευτέρᾳ γενέσει
  - θήρειον φύσιν
  - λόγῳ κρατήσας
  - εἶδος ἕξεως
  - διαθεσμοθετήσας
english_gloss: second birth, changes, beastly nature, mastered by reason, form of condition, legislated
observation: "The demiurge legislates a reincarnation sequence: a soul that persists in vice undergoes transformation into progressively lower beastly natures (42c), and only by overcoming the irrational accretions from fire, water, air, and earth through reason can it return to its original best form of condition (42c-42d)."
textual_basis: At 42c the text links a second birth to moral failure, names a 'beastly nature' as the terminus of repeated transformation, and specifies rational mastery as the means to regain the first and best 'form of condition'. The demiurge's legislative act brackets this as a cosmic law.
limits: This observation identifies a bounded mythic account of soul transmigration as a textual phenomenon. It does not claim that the passage is esoteric, that it encodes a hidden doctrine, or that it represents a specific historical Pythagorean influence.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0116
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 42d-42e

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 42d-42e
  start_marker: 42d
  end_marker: 42e
  start_char: 45480
  end_char: 46358
  text_sha256: 2afdbf34d06c559e65dbfb441a758c4566121ad70b3b114631c10216285d7870
greek_terms:
  - ἔσπειρεν
  - τοὺς μὲν εἰς γῆν, τοὺς δ’ εἰς σελήνην, τοὺς δ’ εἰς τἆλλα
  - ὄργανα χρόνου
  - σώματα πλάττειν θνητά
english_gloss: sowed some into earth, some into moon, some into the other instruments of time, fashion mortal bodies, human soul
observation: "After legislating the conditions for mortal souls, the demiurge sows souls into distinct cosmic locations: earth, moon, and the other instruments of time. He then delegates to the younger gods the task of fashioning mortal bodies and completing the remaining human-soul component."
textual_basis: At 42d-42e the sowing verb distributes souls across three named destinations, and immediately after the sowing the text assigns to the younger gods the work of fashioning mortal bodies, adding what still needed to be added of human soul, and governing the mortal living thing as well as possible.
limits: This observation records a textual distribution-and-delegation pattern within the mythic narrative. It does not assert that the passage proves a doctrine of astral determinism or that the demiurge's sowing is esoteric.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0117
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 42c-42d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 42c-42d
  start_marker: 42c
  end_marker: 42d
  start_char: 45132
  end_char: 45878
  text_sha256: 255991bc3b701225faca13584d8ca922b91163bd17d32e10151623b5140d11db
greek_terms:
  - διαθεσμοθετήσας
  - ἀναίτιος
english_gloss: legislated, not responsible, of subsequent vice
observation: "The demiurge's act of legislating all these arrangements is explicitly motivated by a desire to be 'not responsible' for any soul's subsequent vice (42d). This provides a causal-priority argument: the maker establishes the initial conditions and law but the moral failure that triggers the reincarnation cycle belongs to the individual soul, not to the divine cause."
textual_basis: "At 42d the purpose clause states that the legislation ensures the demiurge is not the cause of each soul's subsequent vice. The causal priority is explicit: the initial sowing and law-giving are complete before any soul can become vicious, making the maker causally prior but morally exonerated."
limits: This observation records an explicit causal attribution and exoneration claim within a mythic passage. It does not claim that this is a hidden proof or that the passage operates as a formal syllogism about divine responsibility.
review_status: rejected
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0118
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 40c-40d

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

greek_terms:
  - φύλακα
  - δημιουργὸν
english_gloss: guardian and craftsman
observation: At 40c the earth is given the title of guardian and craftsman of night and day, transferring craft/professional language to a cosmic body.
textual_basis: Timaeus describes the earth as "a guardian and craftsman of night and day" (40c), using the same noun demiourgos that appears elsewhere for divine craftsmanship. The epithet is applied to the earth itself, a generated cosmic body within the heaven.
limits: This observation does not claim that the earth is literally a craftsman; it only registers the transfer of craft vocabulary to a non-human cosmic entity. The passage treats the earth as the first and most venerable among the generated gods, not as a metaphor for human skill.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0119
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 40c-40d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

  start_marker: 40c

  start_char: 41088
  end_char: 42056
  text_sha256: 350e53bc587c7071bb2fa20470e1b931bd5ee6b51cb2b87508d8f3a0f0244bcc
greek_terms:
  - εἰρημένα
  - φύσεως
  - ἐχέτω
  - τέλος
english_gloss: let what has been said about nature have its end
observation: At the end of 40c-40d the speaker uses a formal closure formula to close the discussion of the visible and generated gods, marking an explicit structural break before turning to the topic of daimons.
textual_basis: The text at 40d states "let what has been said about the visible and generated gods by nature have its end" (eiremena physeos echeto telos). This is a self-conscious structural marker that closes one section of the cosmological account before the transition to "the other daimons."
limits: This observation records a surface textual structure marker, not a claim about the philosophical content of the preceding section. The formula indicates a speaker-controlled division of the discourse, not necessarily a doctrinal closure.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0120
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 40c-40d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 40c-40d
  start_marker: 40c
  end_marker: 40d
  start_char: 41088
  end_char: 42056
  text_sha256: 350e53bc587c7071bb2fa20470e1b931bd5ee6b51cb2b87508d8f3a0f0244bcc
greek_terms:
  - δαιμόνων
  - ἐκγόνοις
  - θεῶν
english_gloss: daimons, descendants of gods
observation: At 40d the speaker marks a boundary between the account of the visible generated gods and the account of daimons by declaring that daimon genealogy is beyond human capacity and must be received from earlier traditional authorities who claimed descent from the gods.
textual_basis: The speaker says at 40d that speaking about and knowing the generation of the other daimons is "greater than is in our power" and that "we must trust those who have spoken before, who were descendants of gods, as they said, and presumably knew their own ancestors clearly." This hedges the daimon account behind traditional genealogy rather than cosmological demonstration.
limits: This observation notes a demarcation device, not a claim that Plato rejects the daimon account. The passage explicitly trusts the earlier speakers; the observation only records the structural boundary and the appeal to inherited tradition as the basis for what follows.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0121
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 42a-42b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 42a-42b
  start_marker: 42a
  end_marker: 42b
  start_char: 44440
  end_char: 45132
  text_sha256: b504052077c8ddc98370eb7bf60fcd2915c60405b5af6e1dc40579077678ac50
greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: "At 42a-b the Demiurge's speech enumerates a list of psychic affections that become innate when souls are implanted in bodies: sensation from violent impressions, then eros mixed with pleasure and pain, and in addition fear, spiritedness, and their accompaniments and contraries. Mastery of these yields a life of justice; being mastered yields injustice."
textual_basis: The passage at 42a-b catalogues 'pleasure', 'pain', 'eros', 'fear', 'thumos' and their cognates as innate affections arising from embodiment, then ties the ethical outcome (justice vs. injustice) to whether the soul masters or is mastered by these affections. This maps a typology of character outcomes onto a psychic-affective taxonomy.
limits: This observation records the catalogue of innate affections and the mastery/mastered ethical binary; it does not assess whether this maps onto Republic tripartition or any later psychological theory.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0122
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 42a-42b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 42a-42b
  start_marker: 42a
  end_marker: 42b
  start_char: 44440
  end_char: 45132
  text_sha256: b504052077c8ddc98370eb7bf60fcd2915c60405b5af6e1dc40579077678ac50
greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: At 42a-b the Demiurge's address provides an aetiology of the difference between male and female embodied natures. The twofold human nature is described, with the stronger kind being that which would later be called 'man' (aner); souls that fail in the mastery of affections are said to pass into a woman's nature in a second birth.
textual_basis: The passage states that human nature is double (diples...tes anthropines phuseos) and the stronger kind would later be called 'aner' at 42a; at 42b it says that one who stumbles (sphaleis) in mastering the affections passes into a woman's nature (eis gunaikos phusin) in the next birth. This provides an aetiological mythic account of sexual differentiation.
limits: The observation notes the aetiological function of the Demiurge's speech regarding sex differentiation and reincarnation; it does not evaluate the content as gender ideology or compare it with other Platonic treatments of gender.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0123
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 42a-42b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 42a-42b
  start_marker: 42a
  end_marker: 42b
  start_char: 44440
  end_char: 45132
  text_sha256: b504052077c8ddc98370eb7bf60fcd2915c60405b5af6e1dc40579077678ac50
greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: "At 42b the Demiurge narrates a reincarnation schema tied to ethical character: one who lives well for the appointed time returns to the habitation of his companion star and enjoys a happy and congenial life; one who fails passes into a woman's nature in the next birth."
textual_basis: The text at 42b states that the one who lives well (eu...bious) for the fitting time returns to the dwelling of his companion star (sunonomou...oikesin astron) and has a blessed and familiar life (bion eudaimona kai sunethe), while the one who stumbles (sphaleis) passes into a woman's nature. This is a reincarnation outcome mapped to ethical desert.
limits: This observation records the reincarnation-by-character taxonomy in the Demiurge's speech; it does not assess its consistency with other Platonic afterlife myths (e.g., Phaedo, Republic X, Phaedrus).
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0124
source_work: Timaeus

greek_terms:
  - αἰσθήσεις
english_gloss: the six motions ... sense-perceptions
observation: At 43b-43c the embodied soul's motions are described as disordered and irrational, moving in all six directions (forward/backward, right/left, up/down), and the flux of incoming bodily affections is explicitly identified as the origin of sense-perceptions (aisthēseis).
textual_basis: The passage catalogs the six directional motions (43b) and then traces how external impacts on the body produce movements that strike the soul, stating that these were therefore called and still are called aisthēseis (43c).
limits: The observation records the naming of sense-perception as a consequence of embodiment; it does not evaluate whether this account is consistent with earlier passages about the soul's composition.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: At 43b the influx of nourishment is described through an image of a flooding and ebbing wave (kataklyzontos kai aporreontos kymatos), and this wave produces even greater disturbance in the soul.
textual_basis: "The wave image is directly stated at 43b: 'for the wave that was flooding in and ebbing out, which provided nourishment, produced still greater disturbance.' The disturbance is explictly linked to bodily affections striking the soul."
limits: The observation notes the wave imagery for bodily influx; it does not analyze whether this image is consistent with the earlier construction of the soul from the circle of the Same and the Different.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0126

  - ἀκήρατα
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Demiurge's second mixing in the bowl produces soul-stuff that is explicitly graded as "second and third" (deutera kai trita), in contrast to the earlier pure mixture used for the world-soul.
textual_basis: At 41d, after addressing the lesser gods, the Demiurge returns to the mixing bowl (krater) and pours in the remnants of the previous mixture. The text states the mixture is no longer pure in the same way but "second and third" (akerata de ouketi kata tauta hosautos, alla deutera kai trita).
limits: This observes the textual claim of a graded mixture; it does not interpret the metaphysical status of second- and third-grade soul-stuff or its implications for the hierarchy of souls.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0127
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 41d-41e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 41d-41e
  start_marker: 41d
  end_marker: 41e
  start_char: 43707
  end_char: 44440
  text_sha256: 653ee59f654ca88321228dd9ee3f9a7add5082378f7133442c1682e00ab77a3d
greek_terms:
  - προσυφαίνοντες
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Demiurge instructs the lesser gods to fashion living beings by "weaving mortal onto immortal" (athanato thneton prosuphainontes).
textual_basis: At 41d, the Demiurge addresses the lesser gods with the instruction to weave mortal onto immortal as they fashion living beings (to de loipon humeis, athanato thneton prosuphainontes, apergazesthe zoia).
limits: This notes the weaving metaphor as a craft image; it does not determine whether the weaving refers to the conjunction of soul and body or to another compositional act.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0128
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 41d-41e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

  text_sha256: 653ee59f654ca88321228dd9ee3f9a7add5082378f7133442c1682e00ab77a3d
greek_terms:
  - γένεσις πρώτη
  - τεταγμένη μία πᾶσιν
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Demiurge decrees that the first birth (genesis prote) will be one and the same for all souls, so that no soul is disadvantaged by him.
textual_basis: At 41e, the Demiurge speaks the fated laws (nomous tous heimarmenous) to the souls, declaring that the first birth would be one and the same for all, so that no soul would be disadvantaged by him (genesis prote men esoito tetagmene mia pasin, hina metis elattoito hup' autou).
limits: This observes the egalitarian principle stated in the mythic decree; it does not address what the first birth consists of or how subsequent births differ.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0129
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 44a-44b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 44a-44b
  start_marker: 44a
  end_marker: 44b
  start_char: 48492
  end_char: 49404
  text_sha256: c1db828335fa81764b14c40cd8e3c9b814498fdafb6a999251e9ff21cfc60a4c
greek_terms:
  - ταὐτόν
  - θάτερον
  - περίοδοι
english_gloss: ""
observation: The soul's circles of the Same and the Different produce false judgments when disrupted by external sensations, calling things same or different contrary to the truth (44a).
textual_basis: At 44a, the text states that when the circles encounter external sensations belonging to the genus of the Same or the Different, they call a thing same as something and different from something contrary to the truths, and become false and mindless.
limits: This observation records the cognitive function attributed to the circles of Same and Different within the Timaeus's cosmological psychology. It does not evaluate whether this account is presented as a likely account or as demonstrative proof.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0130
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 44a-44b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 44a-44b
  start_marker: 44a
  end_marker: 44b
  start_char: 48492
  end_char: 49404
  text_sha256: c1db828335fa81764b14c40cd8e3c9b814498fdafb6a999251e9ff21cfc60a4c
greek_terms:
  - ἄνους
  - ἔμφρονα
  - ἐνδεθῇ θνητόν
english_gloss: ""
observation: The soul becomes mindless at first when bound into a mortal body, and gradually becomes intelligent as the stream of growth and nourishment lessens and its circles stabilize with time.
textual_basis: At 44a-44b, the text states that the soul becomes mindless at first when bound into a mortal body. It then describes how, as the growth-and-nourishment stream lessens and the circles gain calm and stabilize over time, the revolutions are corrected and the soul's possessor becomes intelligent.
limits: This observation records the developmental claim about the soul's cognitive state upon embodiment within the Timaeus's likely account. It does not include the following alternatives for proper education or neglect, and it does not compare this claim with accounts of soul in other dialogues.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0131
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 30d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 30d
  start_marker: 30d
  end_marker: 30d
  start_char: 25448
  end_char: 25626
  text_sha256: 8ff7cd3130c162ed5ebab486074a899f7bc6d1a4e4bf212dd19c31260ee5ca24
greek_terms:
  - τῷ γὰρ τῶν νοουμένων καλλίστῳ
  - κατὰ πάντα τελέῳ
english_gloss: ""
observation: At 30d, Timaeus states that the god wished to make the cosmos like the fairest and most perfect of intelligible things, establishing the demiurge's act of looking to an intelligible model as the explanatory frame for cosmic generation.
textual_basis: Timaeus says the god wished to liken the cosmos to the fairest and most perfect of intelligible things. The verb for wishing ascribes a deliberate intention, and the superlatives fairest and most perfect identify the model as the best within the intelligible domain, setting up the craft analogy that structures the remainder of the cosmogony.
limits: This observation records only the explicit statement that the demiurge looked to an intelligible paradigm. It does not claim that the paradigm is the Form of the Good, nor does it address whether the demiurge is a literal craftsman or a metaphorical figure.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0132
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 30d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 30d
  start_marker: 30d
  end_marker: 30d

  end_char: 25626
  text_sha256: 8ff7cd3130c162ed5ebab486074a899f7bc6d1a4e4bf212dd19c31260ee5ca24
greek_terms:
  - ζῷον ἓν ὁρατόν
english_gloss: ""
observation: At 30d, the cosmos is described as a single visible living thing made in likeness of the intelligible model, introducing a visible/intelligible contrast that anticipates form-participation language.
textual_basis: The phrase for single visible living thing directly characterizes the cosmos as a visible entity. The god's act of likening the cosmos to the intelligible paradigm positions the visible world as an image of the intelligible, which is a recurring pattern in Platonic form-language.
limits: This observation does not claim that the passage uses explicit participation vocabulary or that the cosmos is said to be a Form. It notes only the visible/intelligible contrast and the image relation established at 30d.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0133
source_work: Timaeus

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

  text_sha256: 08093548151e84a06b3f2f7fb13a56656d2c9b18e6a5093006b9cedbe947d156
greek_terms:
  - ἐγκαύματα
  - ἀνεκπλύτου γραφῆς
  - ἔμμονά
  - ἐπανερωτῶντος
english_gloss: ""
observation: Critias explains his readiness to retell the story by comparing repeated childhood questioning and instruction to indelible painted marks that have remained in him.
textual_basis: At 26c, Critias says the things heard as play, with the elder eagerly teaching and the child often asking again, became lasting in him like marks of an unwashable painting; he then says he repeated them to the others that morning.
limits: This records the memory-authorization frame for the reported story. It does not verify the truth of what is remembered.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0134

greek_terms:
  - τἀληθὲς
  - προγόνους
  - πρέπον
  - ἀποδοῦναι
english_gloss: ""
observation: Critias proposes to transfer Socrates' city from the preceding myth-like account onto truth by identifying its citizens with the real ancestors named by the Egyptian priest.
textual_basis: At 26d, Critias says they will place the city from yesterday onto truth, call the imagined citizens the true ancestors spoken of by the priest, and jointly try to render what is fitting for Socrates' assignment.
limits: This records a frame move that reclassifies the city narrative as a true ancestral account. It does not decide whether the identification is historically credible.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0135
source_work: Timaeus

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

greek_terms:
  - εἰκότας
  - γένεσιν
  - οὐσία
  - πίστιν
  - ἀλήθεια
english_gloss: ""
observation: Timaeus sets the standard for cosmological discourse as likelihood rather than exact self-agreement, grounding the weaker standard in the relation of becoming to being and belief to truth.
textual_basis: At 29c, Timaeus distinguishes accounts of an image from accounts of the stable model and asks the audience not to be surprised if discourse about gods and cosmic generation cannot be entirely exact, so long as it provides likely accounts.
limits: This records the explicit likelihood standard for the cosmology. It does not treat likelihood as equivalent to demonstrative truth.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0136

  - φύσιν ἀνθρωπίνην
  - προοίμιον
  - νόμον
english_gloss: ""
observation: Timaeus asks that the likely myth be accepted without seeking beyond it because speaker and judges share human nature; Socrates accepts this rule and asks him to complete the account.
textual_basis: At 29d, Timaeus says human nature should accept the likely myth about these matters and seek nothing further. Socrates responds that the prelude has been accepted and tells Timaeus to proceed with the rule.
limits: This records the procedural acceptance of the likely-account standard. It does not evaluate whether the following account satisfies that standard.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0137
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 31a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

  end_char: 26082
  text_sha256: 5d431384ab7076ab232702f4b1f1f1acbbaf8b0a849138f444e6593156426344
greek_terms:
  - ἕνα οὐρανὸν
  - πολλοὺς
  - ἀπείρους
  - παράδειγμα
  - περιέχον
english_gloss: ""
observation: Timaeus argues that the heaven must be one rather than many or infinite, because the paradigm containing all intelligible living things cannot have a second alongside it.
textual_basis: At 31a, Timaeus asks whether they rightly called the heaven one or should speak of many and infinite heavens, answers that it is one if made according to the paradigm, and reasons that the all-containing intelligible living thing would not be second with another.
limits: This records the uniqueness argument from the paradigm's completeness. It does not include the continuation of the argument in the following span.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0138
source_work: Timaeus

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

  text_sha256: 0d74ab8e4db92ace49d499439e72b510ffb30603166b890b4b2592e24de24d0e
greek_terms:
  - παῖδες
  - πατρὸς τάξιν
  - ἀθάνατον ἀρχὴν
  - θνητοῦ ζῴου
  - μιμούμενοι
  - δημιουργόν
english_gloss: ""
observation: After the demiurge has arranged the mortal condition, the younger gods obey the father's order, receive the immortal beginning of the mortal living being, and imitate their own maker in constructing bodies.
textual_basis: At 42e, the maker remains in his customary state after arranging these things, while his children understand and obey the father's order; they take the immortal beginning of a mortal living being and, imitating their demiurge, begin borrowing elemental materials from the cosmos.
limits: This records the delegated craft sequence. It does not treat the younger gods as independent first causes.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0139

  - σῶμα
  - ἀθανάτου ψυχῆς
  - ἐπίρρυτον
  - ἀπόρρυτον
english_gloss: ""
observation: The younger gods assemble each body from borrowed elemental parts that must later be returned, fastening them with small invisible joints and binding the immortal soul's revolutions into a flowing body.
textual_basis: At 43a, the elements borrowed from the cosmos are to be given back again; the gods join them into one body with small invisible fastenings and bind the periods of the immortal soul into a body described as subject to inflow and outflow.
limits: This records the construction image for embodied life. It does not explain the later disorder of the soul's motions.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

observation: Timaeus sets aside the preceding consequences as later matters and redirects the account to a more exact treatment of the proposed topics, including bodily generation by parts, soul, causes, and divine forethought.
textual_basis: At 44c, Timaeus says the prior consequences come later and that the matters now proposed must be gone through more exactly, naming bodily generation by parts, soul, causes, and the gods' forethought.
limits: This records the procedural redirection only. It does not include the likely-account standard completed in the next marker or the head/body construction that follows.
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0141
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 44d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

greek_terms:
  - θείας περιόδους
  - κεφαλὴν
  - θειότατόν
  - δεσποτοῦν
  - ὑπηρεσίαν
english_gloss: ""
observation: The head is introduced as the spherical enclosure of the divine revolutions, the most divine part and ruler within us, with the rest of the body gathered by the gods for its service.
textual_basis: At 44d, the two divine revolutions are bound into a spherical body imitating the shape of the whole; this is named the head, called most divine and ruling over all within us, and the whole body is assembled as service to it.
limits: This records the cosmological anatomy assigned to the head. It does not claim that the body is merely dispensable.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0142
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 44d-44e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 44d-44e
  start_marker: 44d
  end_marker: 44e
  start_char: 49788
  end_char: 50533
  text_sha256: 18dedfbbb31b3016adb9abe087ec601c6189b2cf16384b85151a75fb64b313f6
greek_terms:
  - ὄχημα
  - εὐπορίαν
  - μῆκος
  - κῶλα
  - πορείαν
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The body is assigned to the head as a vehicle and means of passage: because the head would otherwise roll helplessly over uneven earth, the gods give the body length and four flexible limbs for locomotion."
textual_basis: Across 44d-44e, the head is the antecedent of the problem of rolling over varied ground; the gods then give it a vehicle and ease of passage, and the body receives length and four extended, flexible limbs contrived for movement.
limits: This records the vehicle/locomotion explanation only. It does not duplicate the separate claim that the head is the divine ruling part, and it does not include later organ-specific construction.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0143
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 45a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

  - ἱερωτάτου
  - πρόσθεν
  - ἀρχικώτερον
  - πρόσωπον
  - ὄργανα
english_gloss: ""
observation: The front of the body is made distinct and authoritative because the gods treat forward direction as more honorable and ruling, then attach the face and instruments around the head.
textual_basis: At 45a, the body carries the dwelling of the most divine and sacred part above us; because the gods regard the front as more honorable and ruling than the back, they give most motion forward and set the face and instruments around the head.
limits: This records the teleological placement of face and organs. It does not include the specific mechanism of sight.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0144
source_work: Timaeus

  - φωσφόρα
  - ὄμματα
  - φῶς ἥμερον
  - πῦρ
  - ὀμμάτων
english_gloss: ""
observation: The eyes are constructed as light-bearing organs by channeling a gentle non-burning fire through them as a dense, smooth stream.
textual_basis: At 45b, Timaeus says the gods first fashioned light-bearing eyes and explains that they made the gentle light-giving part of fire become a body; the pure fire within us is made to flow through the eyes.
limits: This records the construction premise for sight. It does not include the external daylight contact described next.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0145
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 45c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 45c

greek_terms:
  - ὄψεως ῥεῦμα
  - ὅμοιον πρὸς ὅμοιον
  - ἓν σῶμα
  - εὐθυωρίαν
english_gloss: ""
observation: Sight is explained as a stream from the eyes meeting kindred daylight and becoming one continuous body along the straight line of vision.
textual_basis: At 45c, when daylight surrounds the stream of sight, like goes toward like, becomes compact, and forms one kindred body in the straight course from the eyes where the inner outgoing fire meets what it falls upon outside.
limits: This records the visual-contact mechanism. It does not describe how the motion reaches the soul, which follows in the next span.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0146
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 45d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 45d
  start_marker: 45d
  end_marker: 45d
  start_char: 51776
  end_char: 52225
  text_sha256: 05a9f6c6da8e05902be3e6fd46f7b94bb6d444716a51d4edc93e31c8f8c04423
greek_terms:
  - κινήσεις
  - ψυχῆς
  - αἴσθησιν
  - ὁρᾶν
  - νύκτα
  - ὕπνου
  - βλεφάρων
english_gloss: ""
observation: The visual body transmits motions through the whole body to the soul as the perception called seeing, but when kindred fire departs into night the process is cut off and becomes an inducement to sleep.
textual_basis: At 45d, Timaeus says contact transmits motions through the whole body to the soul, providing the perception called seeing; when the related fire departs into night, it is altered and extinguished in unlike air, so seeing stops and sleep is induced.
limits: This records the night cutoff of the visual mechanism. It does not include the subsequent dream condition.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0147
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 45d-45e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 45d-45e
  start_marker: 45d
  end_marker: 45e

  end_char: 52527

greek_terms:
  - βλεφάρων
  - πυρὸς ἐντὸς
  - κινήσεις
  - ἡσυχία
  - ὕπνος
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The eyelids are described as a safeguard for sight: when they close, they confine the inner fire, smooth the internal motions, and produce the stillness that brings sleep."
textual_basis: At the 45d-45e boundary, Timaeus names the eyelids as the gods' safeguard for sight; their closing confines the inner fire, which smooths the internal motions, and the smoothing produces stillness and sleep.
limits: This records the eyelid-and-sleep mechanism only. It does not re-record the night cutoff of sight, and it does not include the later account of dream images from remaining motions.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  - λειότητα
english_gloss: ""
observation: Timaeus extends the visual mechanism to mirrors and smooth visible surfaces, explaining image-production through the communion of inner and outer fire at the polished surface.
textual_basis: At 46a, after recalling dreamlike images, Timaeus says the making of images in mirrors and all smooth visible things is no longer hard to understand; the account depends on the inner and outer fire coming together around smoothness and being repeatedly reshaped.
limits: This records the physical explanation of mirrored images. It does not discuss later optical reversals beyond this span.

```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0149

  text_sha256: b4d0dcac0a847dcad588807fa2ec7057411d7a76ccb134f123f32d668933c4aa
greek_terms:
  - ἐμφαίνεται
  - πρόσωπον
  - ὄψιν
  - λεῖον
  - λαμπρὸν
  - δεξιὰ
  - ἀριστερά
english_gloss: ""
observation: Mirror appearance is explained as a necessary result of the face-fire and sight-fire becoming compact around a smooth bright surface, with right and left reversed when opposite parts of vision meet opposite parts.
textual_basis: At 46b, Timaeus says such appearances arise of necessity when the fire around the face and the fire around sight join around the smooth and bright surface; right things appear left because contact occurs with opposite parts rather than the usual arrangement.
limits: This records the lateral reversal mechanism. It does not include the vertical reversal described in the next span.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

greek_terms:
  - κατόπτρων
  - λειότης
  - δεξιὸν
  - ἀριστερὸν
  - ὕπτιον
  - συναιτίων
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The account adds a second mirror case: when the mirror's smoothness is turned lengthwise relative to the face, the image appears inverted by pushing the lower ray upward and the upper ray downward."
textual_basis: At 46c, Timaeus explains that a mirror surface with heights on either side can push right toward left and the other side toward the other; when the same arrangement is turned lengthwise to the face, the whole appears upside down through a lower-to-upper and upper-to-lower displacement.
limits: This records the optical mechanism before the causal-method transition at the end of the span.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0151

  text_sha256: c6b43f792f88ff539e7638ad152c3897510d381dadc1d4d0327cc72bfc3e43e1
greek_terms:
  - συναίτια
  - αἴτια
  - λόγον
  - νοῦν
  - ψυχήν
  - πῦρ
  - ὕδωρ
  - γῆ
  - ἀὴρ
english_gloss: ""
observation: "Timaeus distinguishes auxiliary physical factors from true causes: most people mistake heating, cooling, compacting, and dissolving bodies for causes, but these visible bodies lack reason and mind."
textual_basis: At 46d, Timaeus says the many think such factors are causes rather than co-causes of everything; he then denies that fire, water, earth, and air can possess reason or mind, assigning mind properly to soul rather than to visible bodies.
limits: This records the methodological distinction between causes and co-causes. It does not yet state the full program for treating both kinds separately.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

greek_terms:
  - αἰτιῶν γένη
  - μετὰ νοῦ
  - καλῶν
  - ἀγαθῶν
  - ἄτακτον
  - ὀμμάτων
english_gloss: ""
observation: "Timaeus states the rule for the next account: both kinds of causes must be spoken of, but separately, distinguishing makers of good and beautiful things with mind from necessity-working processes that produce disorderly outcomes when isolated from intelligence."
textual_basis: "At 46e, he says they must speak of both classes of causes separately: those that, with mind, are craftsmen of beautiful and good things, and those that, deprived of understanding, produce whatever chance and disorder bring about. He then closes the account of the eyes' contributing causes and turns to their greatest benefit."
limits: This records the programmatic distinction and transition. It does not describe the full necessity account that begins later.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

greek_terms:
  - ὄψις

  - ἄστρα
  - ἥλιον
  - οὐρανὸν
  - ἀριθμόν
  - χρόνου
  - ζήτησιν
english_gloss: ""
observation: Sight is named as the cause of the greatest human benefit because seeing the stars, sun, and heaven makes possible number, the concept of time, and inquiry into the nature of the whole.
textual_basis: At 47a, Timaeus says none of the present accounts about the whole would have been spoken if humans had not seen the stars, sun, and heaven; seeing day, night, months, years, equinoxes, and solstices has devised number, given an understanding of time, and supplied inquiry into the whole.
limits: This records the stated benefit of vision. It does not yet include the claim about philosophy as the greatest divine gift in the next span.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0154
source_work: Timaeus

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

  end_marker: 47b

  end_char: 55399
  text_sha256: 053ebd03d5277d676b3b54690ff8f8502cc3bc685670be288459b50b36fad9d7
greek_terms:
  - φιλοσοφίας
  - ἀγαθὸν
  - θεῶν
  - ὄψιν
  - νοῦ
  - περιόδους
  - διανοήσεως
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The highest good of the eyes is formulated theologically: the gods gave vision so humans could look at the heavenly revolutions of mind and apply them to the revolutions of human thought."
textual_basis: At 47b, Timaeus says humans acquired philosophy from sight, calling it the greatest good ever given to mortals by the gods. He then states that god discovered and gave vision so that humans might observe the heavenly periods of mind and use them for the cycles of their own thinking.
limits: This records the teleological statement about vision and philosophy. It does not identify philosophy with astronomy alone.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0155
source_work: Timaeus

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 47c
  start_marker: 47c
  end_marker: 47c
  start_char: 55399
  end_char: 55793
  text_sha256: a752953aa9a04010a385dfe47a796e57b98de12983d757d69f722e1a4d0c9116
greek_terms:
  - ἀταράκτοις
  - τεταραγμένας
  - λογισμῶν
  - μιμούμενοι
  - ἀπλανεῖς
  - πεπλανημένας
  - φωνῆς
  - ἀκοῆς
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The benefit of observing the heavens is corrective imitation: by learning the untroubled divine revolutions and sharing in correct calculation, humans can stabilize the wandering revolutions within themselves; voice and hearing are then assigned the same general purpose."
textual_basis: At 47c, Timaeus contrasts the untroubled heavenly cycles with the troubled cycles of human thought and says that, by learning and imitating the divine unerring cycles, humans may set right their own wandering ones. He then says the same account applies to voice and hearing, given by the gods for the same ends.
limits: This records the corrective structure of the teleology. It does not include the more specific role of harmony and rhythm in the next spans.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0156

greek_terms:
  - φωνῇ

  - ἡδονὴν ἄλογον
  - κατακόσμησιν
  - συμφωνίαν
english_gloss: ""
observation: Harmony is presented as useful not for irrational pleasure but as an ally for bringing the disordered revolution of the soul into order and concord with itself.
textual_basis: At 47d, Timaeus says what is useful in voice for hearing is given for the sake of harmony. Harmony has motions akin to the soul's inner periods and, for one who uses the Muses with mind, is not for irrational pleasure but for ordering and bringing into concord the disharmonious soul-period within us.
limits: This records the explicit anti-pleasure and soul-ordering account of harmony. It does not treat all musical pleasure as rejected.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

english_gloss: ""
observation: Timaeus closes the account of what has been fashioned through intellect and announces that what comes to be through necessity must now be set beside it in the account.
textual_basis: At 47e, after the preceding account is marked as mostly complete, Timaeus contrasts things made through intellect with things that come to be through necessity and says the latter must be set alongside in the logos.
limits: This records the methodological transition only. It does not preserve the preceding rhythm-as-helper clause, and it does not state the following claim that intellect persuades necessity.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  - ἀνομοιότατα
  - ὁμοιότατα
  - ἀλόγως
  - ἀμέτρως
english_gloss: ""
observation: Before cosmic ordering, the receptacle's shaking motion separates the four kinds by likeness and unlikeness, so similar things are pushed together and dissimilar things are set apart while everything remains without reason and measure.
textual_basis: At 53a, Timaeus says the four kinds are shaken by the receptacle as by an instrument that supplies motion; the most unlike are separated from each other, the most alike are driven together, and before the ordered whole comes to be these things are in different places and are without reason and measure.
limits: This records the pre-cosmic sorting mechanism. It does not identify the four kinds with fully ordered elements yet.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

english_gloss: ""
observation: When the whole begins to be ordered, the god first shapes fire, water, earth, and air, which had only traces of themselves, by forms and numbers so far as possible into the finest and best arrangement.
textual_basis: At 53b, Timaeus says that when ordering was undertaken, the four kinds had some traces of themselves but were disposed as things are when god is absent; the god first shaped them by forms and numbers and established them as beautifully and excellently as possible from a prior disorderly condition.
limits: This records the mathematizing craft premise. It does not provide the subsequent geometric construction itself.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0160
source_work: Timaeus

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

greek_terms:
  - παίδευσιν
  - σώματά
  - βάθος
  - ἐπίπεδον
  - τρίγωνα
english_gloss: ""
observation: Timaeus begins the unusual mathematical account by reducing the visible bodies to depth, depth to plane surface, and plane bases to triangles.
textual_basis: At 53c, after saying the hearers can follow because they share in the paths of education, Timaeus states that fire, earth, water, and air are bodies; every bodily form has depth, depth necessarily includes plane nature, and a right plane base is composed from triangles.
limits: This records the first reduction in the geometric construction. It does not yet select the two fundamental triangles.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  - εἰκότα λόγον
english_gloss: ""
observation: The construction posits two right triangles as the immediate principles of fire and the other bodies, while marking any higher principles as known only to god and a friend of god.
textual_basis: At 53d, Timaeus distinguishes two right triangles, one with equal sides dividing the right angle equally and one with unequal divisions; he says these are hypothesized as the principle of fire and the other bodies while proceeding by the likely account with necessity.
limits: This records the local geometric hypothesis. It does not claim access to the higher principles that the text explicitly withholds.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

english_gloss: ""
observation: The stated task is to construct four most beautiful unlike bodies that can, at least in some cases, dissolve into and come to be from one another, thereby grasping the generation of earth, fire, and the proportional intermediates.
textual_basis: At 53e, Timaeus asks what four most beautiful bodies could be unlike one another yet capable of becoming from one another when dissolved; he says that if they find this they have the truth about the generation of earth, fire, and the middle proportional bodies.
limits: This records the constructive goal. It does not yet determine which body belongs to which element.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0163

  - πρόμηκες
  - ἀπεράντους
  - κάλλιστον
  - ἰσόπλευρον τρίγωνον
english_gloss: ""
observation: Among infinitely many elongated triangles, Timaeus selects the finest triangle for the construction, while inviting anyone with a better selection to prevail as a friend rather than an enemy.
textual_basis: At 54a, the isosceles triangle is said to have one nature, while the elongated triangle has unlimited forms; the account therefore chooses the finest among the unlimited candidates, the one from which the equilateral triangle is composed.
limits: This records the selection rule and its open challenge. It does not supply the longer proof that Timaeus says would be required.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0164

  - διοριστέον
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The account fixes two selected triangles for the bodies and immediately corrects an earlier impression: not all four kinds transform through one another in the same way."
textual_basis: At 54b, Timaeus says two triangles have been chosen from which the body of fire and the other bodies have been contrived, one isosceles and one with the greater side triple in square of the lesser; he then says the earlier claim about all four kinds seeming to arise through one another must be more sharply distinguished.
limits: This records the clarification of transformability. It does not yet state the full three-versus-one distinction completed in the next span.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  - σχήματα
english_gloss: ""
observation: Three of the four elemental bodies are intertransformable because they arise from the same unequal-sided triangle, whereas the fourth alone is fitted together from the isosceles triangle.
textual_basis: "At 54c, Timaeus says that the four kinds come from the selected triangles: three from the one with unequal sides and the fourth alone from the isosceles triangle. Thus all cannot dissolve into one another, but the three can, since larger bodies can be broken into many small ones and many small ones can combine into one larger form."
limits: This records the structural reason for restricted transformation. It does not yet name the elements assigned to each solid.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  - διαζωγραφῶν
english_gloss: ""
observation: The fourth solid is identified as cubical with six square faces, and the remaining fifth construction is reserved for the god's use in decorating the whole.
textual_basis: At 55c, six square planes fitted together complete eight solid angles and produce a cubic body with six equilateral square bases. Timaeus then says that since one fifth construction remained, god used it for the whole in decorating it.
limits: This records the cube and fifth-solid notice. It does not decide the following question about one or many worlds.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

greek_terms:

  - ἀέρα

english_gloss: ""
observation: After rejecting infinite worlds as an unlimited sort of opinion and keeping one cosmos according to the likely account, Timaeus begins assigning the generated solids to fire, earth, water, and air, giving the cubical form to earth.
textual_basis: At 55d, Timaeus says infinite worlds would be the opinion of someone lacking due experience, treats the question of one or five worlds as a reasonable perplexity, then states that his likely account indicates one cosmos and turns to distributing the generated kinds among fire, earth, water, and air; the cubical form is assigned to earth.
limits: This records the transition from solid construction to elemental assignment. It does not give all four assignments in this span.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

observation: Earth receives the cube because it is the least mobile and most moldable of the four kinds, and the square-based construction is presented as the most stable by necessity.
textual_basis: At 55e, Timaeus explains the earth assignment by saying earth is the most unmoved of the four kinds and most plastic among bodies. The solid with the safest bases must be of this sort, and the square plane generated from equal-sided triangles stands more stably than the equilateral triangle by parts and as a whole.
limits: This records the stated reason for assigning the cube to earth. It does not include the assignments of the remaining elements.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0173
source_work: Timaeus

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

  end_char: 56835
  text_sha256: b5417eed3872ad10ad80980fd5eed7b7705bfd8dda1b366e09ae55a8a6a520e5
greek_terms:
  - ἀνάγκης
  - νοῦ
  - πείθειν
  - πλανωμένης
  - αἰτίας
english_gloss: ""
observation: The renewed causal account states that the cosmos was generated from a composition of necessity and intellect, with intellect ruling necessity by persuasion and directing most generated things toward the best.
textual_basis: At 48a, Timaeus says the world's genesis came from necessity and intellect, then characterizes intellect as ruling necessity by persuading it to lead most generated things toward the best. The same marker adds that an account of how the cosmos came to be must mix in the wandering kind of cause.
limits: This records the stated causal pairing and persuasion relation. It does not explain the later mechanics of necessity or identify every wandering cause.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  - στοιχεῖα
english_gloss: ""
observation: Timaeus explicitly backs the discourse up to another beginning, saying the account must restart from a different principle and examine fire, water, air, and earth before the generation of heaven.
textual_basis: At 48b, he says they must go back and take another appropriate beginning, then begin again from the beginning with these matters. The required subject is the nature and prior conditions of fire, water, air, and earth before the heaven's generation.
limits: This records the structural restart and its immediate topic. It does not yet supply the promised elemental account.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0175

  - χαλεπὸν
  - διεξόδου
  - ῥητέον
english_gloss: ""
observation: The restart is bounded by a refusal to state the first principle or principles of all things, because the present mode of exposition cannot make such claims clear.
textual_basis: At 48c, Timaeus says that, for now, they should not speak about whether there is one principle, many principles, or some other account of these things. The reason given is the difficulty of showing what seems right in the present manner of exposition.
limits: This records a procedural deferral of first-principle claims. It does not deny that such principles exist or that they could be discussed elsewhere.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0176

  - ἀτόπου

  - διηγήσεως
english_gloss: ""
observation: Timaeus preserves the earlier rule of likely accounts while preparing an unusual narrative, promising to speak with at least as much likelihood as before and invoking divine aid for the account.
textual_basis: At 48d, he says he will guard the power of likely accounts stated at the beginning and attempt to speak about particulars and the whole in likely terms. He also calls on god at the start to preserve them through a strange and unfamiliar narration toward the likely doctrine.
limits: This records the likelihood hedge and invocation. It does not claim the account is myth in the same sense as a narrated traditional story.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0177

greek_terms:
  - τρίτον
  - γένος
  - παραδείγματος
  - νοητὸν
  - μίμημα
english_gloss: ""
observation: The new beginning enlarges the earlier twofold division by requiring a third kind alongside the intelligible model and the visible generated copy.
textual_basis: "At 48e, Timaeus says the renewed beginning about the whole must be divided more extensively than the previous one. He recalls the prior two kinds: the intelligible paradigm that always remains the same and its generated visible imitation, then says a third kind must now be shown."
limits: This records the formal enlargement of the schema. It does not yet identify the third kind beyond its announced necessity.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0178

greek_terms:
  - τρίτον
  - εἶδος
  - ὑποδοχὴν
  - γενέσεως
  - τιθήνην
  - χαλεπὸν
english_gloss: ""
observation: The third kind is introduced as difficult and dim, then provisionally named as the receptacle and nurse of all generation.
textual_basis: At 49a, Timaeus says the previous two kinds were once thought sufficient, but the argument now seems to compel an attempt to make a difficult and dim kind apparent in speech. He asks what power and nature it has and answers that it is the receptacle of all generation, like a nurse.
limits: This records the provisional characterization of the third kind. It does not turn the nursing image into an independent doctrine.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0179
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 49b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 49b

  end_marker: 49b
```
