```yaml
observation: "The lung is constructed as a cooling support for the heart: its soft, bloodless, sponge-like structure receives breath and drink, cools the heart's heat, supplies breathing and relief, and lets spiritedness serve reason with less pain when it is at its height."
textual_basis: At 70c-70d, the makers foresee that the heart's leaping and spirited swelling will occur through fire, then implant the lung as a helper. The account specifies the lung's texture, internal channels, cooling function, placement around the heart, and poultice-like role in making spirited service to reason easier.
limits: This records the stated function of the lung in the heart-thumos system. It does not include the appetitive part whose account begins at the end of 70d and continues beyond the requested span.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "The appetitive part is described with a feeding-animal image: appetite for food and drink is lodged in the lower torso, placed near a trough-like region for bodily nourishment, and bound there as a wild creature that must still be fed for a mortal race to exist."
textual_basis: Across 70d-70e, the account identifies the soul-part that desires food and drink and everything the body's nature needs. It locates this part below the diaphragm, describes the region as a trough for bodily nourishment, and calls the part a wild creature whose feeding is necessary for mortal embodiment.
limits: This records the feeding-animal image for appetite. It does not include the liver-divination account or claim appetite is rejected outright.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
  start_marker: 70e
  end_marker: 71a
  start_char: 103145
  end_char: 103945
  text_sha256: b0a0903bb9390238bc4bb162dd3e9c70e03f544d13f3b763c9d12255fd7275c0
greek_terms:
  - πορρωτάτω
  - βουλευομένου
  - θόρυβον
  - βοὴν
  - κράτιστον
  - συμφέροντος
  - βουλεύεσθαι
  - τάξιν
english_gloss: farthest away, deliberating part, noise, shout, best part, advantage, deliberate, station
observation: "The location of appetite is justified by distance from deliberation: it is stationed as far as possible from the deliberating part so that its noise is minimized and the best part can deliberate quietly about common and private advantage."
textual_basis: "At 70e-71a, the passage moves from the feeding-trough placement to an explicit reason for the station: appetite should feed there, live farthest from the deliberating element, provide as little noise and shouting as possible, and thereby allow the best element to deliberate in quiet."
limits: This records the stated anatomical placement and purpose. It does not infer a full political psychology beyond the local contrast between appetite and deliberation.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0267

  end_char: 104393
  text_sha256: 8488505cac80a368fa4adf66514a0dfaf758f00168e2090e6617163552b8889c
greek_terms:
  - λόγου
  - συνήσειν
  - αἰσθήσεως
  - εἰδώλων
  - φαντασμάτων
  - ψυχαγωγήσοιτο
  - ἥπατος
  - κατοίκησιν
english_gloss: reasoned speech, understand, perception, images, appearances, be led, liver, dwelling
observation: "The liver is introduced as a device for reaching a soul part that does not understand reasoned speech: because that part is instead led by images and appearances, god places the liver within its dwelling."
textual_basis: At 71a-71b, the account says this part would not understand reasoned speech and would not naturally care for arguments even if it shared in some perception of them. It then says the part would be led especially by images and appearances by night and day, and on this basis god constructs and places the liver in that dwelling.
limits: This records the stated mediating function of the liver. It does not claim that images are equivalent to rational understanding or that the passage gives a general theory of all imagination.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  - ἧπαρ

  - εἴδωλα
  - πικρότητος
  - ἀπειλῇ
  - χολώδη
  - φαντάσματα
  - γλυκύτητι
english_gloss: liver, mirror, impressions, images, bitterness, threat, bile-colored, appearances, gentleness, sweetness
observation: "The liver is made a mirror-like surface for thoughts from mind, and the passage gives paired affective outcomes: threatening use of bitterness contracts and roughens the liver with painful effects, while gentle images and sweetness straighten, smooth, and free it."
textual_basis: At 71b-71d, the liver is described as dense, smooth, bright, sweet, and bitter, receiving impressions like a mirror. The force from mind can frighten the lower part by using bitterness and threat, producing bile-colored effects, constriction, roughness, pains, and nausea; the opposite appearances from gentle thought quiet the bitterness and make the liver straight, smooth, and free.
limits: This records the local psychophysical mechanism attributed to the liver. It does not treat the described colors and bodily changes as independently verified physiology.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0269

english_gloss: liver, divination, sleep, reasoned speech, intelligence, oracle, divinatory art, unreason, inspired, enthusiasm
observation: "Divination is assigned to the nonrational part around the liver: the passage says this part uses divination in sleep because it lacks reasoned speech and intelligence, and then states that god gave divination to human unreason."
textual_basis: At 71d-71e, the account says the soul part settled around the liver has moderate nighttime conduct and uses divination in sleep because it does not share in reasoned speech or intelligence. It then explains the liver-oracle as a way for the inferior part to touch truth and states that divinatory art was given by god to human unreason, with true inspired divination occurring when ordinary intelligence is blocked in sleep, disease, or enthusiasm.
limits: This records the passage's placement and condition for divination. It does not claim that all forms of human inquiry are irrational or that the dialogue endorses every divinatory practice.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  - κρίνειν

english_gloss: to understand, intelligent person, dream, waking, appearances, calculation, signify, mad person, judge, moderate person
observation: The task of interpreting mantic dreams, waking visions, and appearances is assigned to the intelligent and moderate person, who uses calculation to distinguish what they signify, while the person still in madness is said not to be able to judge his own visions and utterances.
textual_basis: At 71e-72a, the passage says it belongs to an intelligent person to remember and consider what is said or seen in dreams or waking under mantic and enthusiastic conditions, and to divide by calculation what each thing signifies for future, past, or present good or bad. It then says that the one still mad should not judge what appeared or was spoken by himself, and invokes the saying that only the moderate person can act and know himself.
limits: This records the division between inspired reception and rational interpretation. It does not specify a fixed procedure for interpreting each image.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "The passage distinguishes prophets from seers by function: custom appoints prophets as judges over inspired divinations, and the text says they are interpreters of riddling report and appearance rather than the actual seers."
textual_basis: At 72a-72b, after denying that the mad person can judge his own visions and utterances, the passage says the class of prophets is customarily set as judges over inspired divinations. It adds that some call them seers in ignorance, because they are interpreters of riddling report and appearance and should most justly be called prophets of those who divine.
limits: This records a naming and role distinction within the divination passage. It does not describe a civic institution beyond the stated custom.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "The closing note ties the liver's nature and location to divination and contrasts living with dead signs: while the person is alive the liver has clearer signs, but once deprived of life it is blind and its divinations are dimmer."
textual_basis: At 72b, the passage summarizes that the liver has this nature and position for the sake of divination. It then says the liver has clearer signs while each person is living, but becomes blind when deprived of life and has dimmer divinations.
limits: This records the stated contrast between living and dead liver signs. It does not extend the account to the spleen passage beginning after 72b.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0273

  - σπληνὸς
  - καθαίρουσα
  - νόσους
english_gloss: organ, left side, bright, clean, mirror, spleen, cleansing, diseases
observation: "The spleen is explained by its service to the neighboring liver: it is placed to the left so that the liver can remain bright and clean like a prepared mirror, and it receives impurities that arise around the liver during bodily diseases."
textual_basis: At 72c-72d, the account says the neighboring organ is located on the left for the liver's sake, to keep it bright and clean like a mirror and prepared impression surface. It then says that when impurities arise around the liver because of bodily diseases, the porous spleen receives and cleanses them, swelling when filled and shrinking again when the body is cleansed.
limits: This records the teleological placement and cleansing function assigned to the spleen. It does not extend the liver-mirror account beyond the cited maintenance relation.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
english_gloss: soul, mortal, divine, separately, true, god, likely, must risk
observation: "After the account of mortal and divine soul-parts and their separate housing, Timaeus marks the claim as only likely for human speakers: full truth could be asserted only with divine agreement, while the present account must risk saying that it is likely."
textual_basis: At 72d, the summary names what has been said about the soul, its mortal and divine portions, and how, with what companions, and why they were housed separately. It then distinguishes the true account, which could be strongly maintained only if god agreed, from the likely account that the speakers must venture to affirm.
limits: This records the explicit epistemic boundary on the preceding soul anatomy. It does not decide whether any single anatomical detail is true or false.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0275

  - ἐντέρων
  - τροφὴ
  - γαστριμαργίαν
  - ἀφιλόσοφον
  - ἄμουσον
english_gloss: body, reasoning, drinks, foods, lack of discipline, belly, intestines, food, gluttony, unphilosophical, unmusical
observation: "The lower belly and coiled intestines are given an appetite-regulating purpose: the makers foresee excessive use of food and drink, so they create a receptacle and slow passage for nourishment to prevent rapid renewed hunger, gluttony, and a wholly unphilosophical and unmusical mortal kind."
textual_basis: At 72e-73a, Timaeus turns from the soul account to the remaining bodily construction. He says the makers knew future human lack of discipline in food and drink and foresaw that excess beyond the moderate and necessary would occur. They therefore set the lower belly as a receptacle and coiled the intestines so food would not pass through quickly, force the body to need more food again, and produce greediness that makes the whole kind unphilosophical, unmusical, and disobedient to the most divine part in us.
limits: This records the stated teleology of digestive delay. It does not infer a general doctrine of asceticism beyond the cited regulation of food and drink.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
english_gloss: bones, flesh, marrow, bonds, soul, body, triangles, fire, water, air, earth, proportional
observation: The account makes marrow the starting point for bones, flesh, and related bodily structures because the bonds of life, binding soul with body, are rooted there; god selects the straightest and smoothest elemental triangles and mixes them proportionally to make marrow as a seed-mixture for mortal kinds.
textual_basis: At 73b-73c, Timaeus says the genesis of marrow is the starting point for bones, flesh, and the whole such nature. He explains that the bonds of life, by which soul is bound together with body, are rooted through marrow in the mortal kind, and then describes god selecting from the elemental kinds the first straight and smooth triangles, separating them from their kinds, and mixing them proportionally to make marrow as a universal seed-mixture for mortal kinds.
limits: This records the stated construction sequence and bond function of marrow. It does not identify the precise mathematical ratios of the mixture.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0277
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 73c-73d

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

  text_sha256: 99858c019573af39650d8ae24200002f0ff2778b14346e4f9ac1b94b438b72d6
greek_terms:
  - ψυχῶν
  - σχήματα
  - διανομῇ
  - θεῖον σπέρμα
  - ἄρουραν
  - ἐγκέφαλον
  - θνητὸν
  - μυελὸν
  - δεσμοὺς
english_gloss: souls, shapes, distribution, divine seed, field, brain, mortal, marrow, bonds
observation: "Marrow is divided according to the kinds and shapes of soul it will receive: the rounded portion that will hold the divine seed is named brain because the head will enclose it, while the remaining rounded and oblong portions are assigned to the mortal soul and become anchor-points for soul-bonds throughout the body."
textual_basis: At 73c-73d, god plants the kinds of souls in marrow and immediately divides marrow into as many shapes as the soul-kinds and bodily forms require. The part intended to contain the divine seed, like a field, is shaped round and named brain because the head will be its vessel; the remaining parts that will hold the mortal soul are divided into round and oblong shapes and are all called marrow, from which bonds of the whole soul are cast like anchors around the body.
limits: This records the anatomical distribution of marrow and brain. It does not treat the agricultural and anchor images as more than the passage's stated explanatory figures.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "Bone is described as a manufactured material: clean smooth earth is kneaded and moistened with marrow, repeatedly passed through fire and water, and made unmeltable by both before being used to turn a bony sphere around the brain."
textual_basis: At 73e, Timaeus says bone is constituted by sifting clean smooth earth, kneading and moistening it with marrow, then placing it into fire, dipping it into water, and moving it many times between the two until it becomes unmeltable by either. He then says this material is used around the brain to turn a bony sphere, with a narrow passage left.
limits: This records the recipe-like material account of bone and its first use around the brain. It does not include the continuation after 73e about the rest of the skeleton.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
english_gloss: neck, spinal marrow, vertebrae, pivots, seed, stony, joints, motion, bending
observation: "The vertebral structure is explained as both protection and articulation: the maker encloses the neck and spinal marrow in vertebrae like pivots, preserves the whole seed in a stony enclosure, and inserts joints with a mediating power for motion and bending."
textual_basis: At 74a, after the bony sphere around the brain, Timaeus says vertebrae are shaped from bone around the neck and spinal marrow, beginning from the head and extending through the whole trunk. The whole seed is fenced in a stone-like enclosure, and joints are put in it with a middle power for the sake of movement and bending.
limits: This records the stated protective and articulating roles of vertebrae and joints. It does not include the following account of flesh and sinews.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "Sinews are introduced to make the bony body bend and extend: they bind the limbs and, by tightening and relaxing around pivots, allow the body to bend and stretch."
textual_basis: At 74b, after the account notes the stiffness of bony nature, the makers devise sinews. The cited span says sinews bind all the limbs and, by tightening and relaxing around the pivots, provide bending and extension for the body.
limits: This records the sinew-motion function only. It does not include the flesh-protection account, internal moisture, or the material recipe that follows.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

english_gloss: wax-modeler, water, fire, earth, sour, salty, flesh, sinews, bone, mixture, yellow
observation: "The maker is figured as a wax-modeler who gives flesh and sinews distinct recipes: flesh is mixed from water, fire, earth, and a sour-salty ferment, while sinew is blended from bone and unfermented flesh into a middle power with a yellow color."
textual_basis: At 74c-74d, after thinking through the protective functions, the wax-modeler mixes and fits together water, fire, and earth, adds a ferment compounded from sour and salty, and produces juicy soft flesh. The nature of sinews is then mixed from bone and unfermented flesh as a single power between the two, using yellow color, so that sinew is tighter and more glutinous than flesh but softer and wetter than bone.
limits: This records the recipe-like construction vocabulary. It does not infer a chemical theory beyond the listed ingredients and contrasts.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0282
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 74e-75a

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

  text_sha256: d4ee01bd5f05647329e4b64af8dcd5ac383eaf71ea14bf85efd178af14788394
greek_terms:
  - ἐμψυχότατα
  - ὀστῶν
  - σαρξίν
  - ἀψυχότατα

  - ἀναισθησίαν

  - διάνοιαν
  - γλώττης
english_gloss: most ensouled, bones, flesh, least ensouled, joints, bendings, lack of perception, harder to remember, thought, tongue
observation: "Flesh is distributed according to soul-presence, movement, and cognition: the most ensouled bones receive least flesh, the least ensouled receive most, and joints receive little flesh unless necessary, so that bodies do not become hard to move or cognition become less perceptive and harder to remember."
textual_basis: At 74e-75a, the account says the bones with most soul are fenced with the least flesh, while those least ensouled within receive the most and densest flesh. At bone junctions, where no necessity requires flesh, only a small amount is grown so it will not obstruct bending and make bodies hard to carry and move; the passage also warns that many dense layers of flesh would produce lack of perception through solidity and make matters of thought harder to remember and duller. The tongue is named as a special flesh case made for perception.
limits: This records the stated distribution rule and its exception. It does not complete the later account of the tongue.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
  stephanus_span: 75a-75b

  end_marker: 75b
  start_char: 111883
  end_char: 112733
  text_sha256: f9f72fca5c5732a4ddae3dc8c8174e29d68f5ab25cae9530a39b9d4dd6e64ff6
greek_terms:
  - φρονήσεως
  - ἔμφρονα
  - αἰσθήσεων
  - ἀνάγκης
  - πυκνὸν ὀστοῦν
  - σάρκα
  - ὀξυήκοον αἴσθησιν
  - κεφαλὴν
english_gloss: intelligence, intelligent, perceptions, necessity, dense bone, flesh, sharp perception, head
observation: "The passage states a necessary incompatibility between maximum protection and sharp perception: dense bone and much flesh cannot coexist with acute perception, even though such a combination around the head would have made human life longer, healthier, and less painful."
textual_basis: At 75a-75b, Timaeus says bones empty of intelligence because of little soul in the marrow are filled with flesh, while intelligent parts receive less, except for special flesh made for perception such as the tongue. He then states that the nature generated and nourished by necessity nowhere admits dense bone, much flesh, and sharp perception together. If these could coincide, the head would especially have had them, giving humans a fleshy, sinewy, strong head and a much longer, healthier, less painful life.
limits: This records the stated tradeoff imposed by necessity. It does not claim that the makers were unable to choose among the available alternatives.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
  - εὐαισθητοτέρα
  - φρονιμωτέρα
  - ἀσθενεστέρα
english_gloss: craftsmen, longer-lived, worse, shorter-lived, better, head, more perceptive, more intelligent, weaker
observation: The makers choose a shorter better life over a longer worse one, leaving the head without dense flesh and sinews because it has no bends; as a result, the head attached to the body is more perceptive and intelligent but much weaker.
textual_basis: At 75b-75c, the makers of human generation calculate whether to produce a longer-lived worse kind or a shorter-lived better kind. They decide that the shorter better life must always be chosen over the longer worse one, and for that reason do not cover the head with dense flesh and sinews, since it has no bends. The conclusion says the head added to the body is therefore more perceptive and more intelligent, but much weaker.
limits: This records the explicit design choice and its stated consequence for the head. It does not include the following continuation on sinews after 75c.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "The mouth is arranged with teeth, tongue, and lips for two ranked functions: the entrance of what is necessary for bodily nourishment and the exit of speech serving intelligence, which is called the best stream."
textual_basis: "At 75d-75e, the account says the arrangers ordered the mouth's power for necessary and best things. It then divides the mouth by direction: what enters gives nourishment to the body, while the outgoing stream of words serves intelligence and is called finest and best among streams."
limits: This records the stated functional division of the mouth. It does not explain the later accounts of speech, taste, or cognition outside the cited span.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
  - ἀναίσθητον
  - σαρκῶν
  - λέμμα

english_gloss: head, bony, seasons, shaded over, deaf, without perception, flesh, residue, skin
observation: "The account repeats the head-design constraint as a choice between two rejected extremes: leaving the head as bare bone would expose it to seasonal excess, while covering it with a crowd of flesh would make it dull and without perception. Skin appears as the residue of flesh-like nature that avoids both extremes."
textual_basis: At 75e-76a, Timaeus says it was not possible to leave the head only bare bone because of seasonal excesses, nor to let it become covered over by many layers of flesh and thereby dull and without perception. The leftover flesh-like material that does not dry out is separated and is called skin.
limits: This records the stated design constraint and the immediate emergence of skin. It does not claim that every later skin or hair detail has the same cause.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
english_gloss: skin, brain, moisture, growing, clothed around, seams, crown, revolutions, nourishment
observation: "Head skin is generated and closed by moisture around the brain: it gathers to itself, grows around the head, is watered under the seams, and closes at the crown, while the number of seams varies with the force of revolutions and nourishment."
textual_basis: At 76a, the separated skin is said to come together because of the moisture around the brain, grow around the head, and be watered upward under the seams until it closes at the crown like a knot. The same marker explains varied seam patterns by the stronger or weaker conflict between the revolutions and nourishment.
limits: This records the local account of head skin and seams. It does not generalize to all skin on the body.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0288

  - κατερριζοῦτο
  - τριχῶν
  - ἱμαντῶδες
  - ψύξεως
english_gloss: skin, fire, moisture, wet, warm, breath, rooted down, hairs, strap-like, cooling
observation: "Hair is generated from a physical sequence involving punctured skin and moisture: the pure wet and warm portion leaves, while the mixed skin-like portion is stretched outward through the puncture, pushed back inward by surrounding breath, rooted under the skin, and made harder and denser by cooling compression."
textual_basis: At 76b-76c, the divine element punctures the skin with fire, moisture moves out through the openings, the unmixed wet and warm portion departs, and the mixed portion from the same materials as skin is carried outward. Because it moves slowly, surrounding breath pushes it back under the skin where it roots; Timaeus then says the hair-kind grows in the skin as a strap-like relative of skin, hardened and condensed by cooling.
limits: This records the described generation of hair. It does not treat hair's protective purpose, which is stated in the following marker, as part of this record.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 76c-76d, after describing hair's growth, the maker uses the stated causes while also intending hair to be a covering instead of flesh around the brain. The passage specifies the purpose as safety, shade, and shelter through summer and winter, with no obstruction to good perception.
limits: This records the stated purpose of head hair. It does not include the subsequent account of nails.

```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 76d, Timaeus describes the structure around the fingers as a threefold mixture of sinew, skin, and bone that dries into one common hard skin. He then states that it is produced by these co-causes but worked by the most responsible thought for the sake of what would come afterward.
limits: This records the local cause and co-cause distinction in the nail account. It does not identify the later beings except where the following span states them.
```

```yaml
observation: Nails are prefigured in the first human formation because the makers know that women and other animals will later come from men and that many creatures will need nails for many uses.
textual_basis: At 76d-76e, after the material account of the hard skin around fingers, Timaeus says the makers knew that women and the other animals would one day come from men and knew that many creatures would need the use of nails for many purposes. For that reason they sketched the generation of nails immediately in humans as they came to be.
limits: This records the future-oriented explanation for nails. It does not evaluate the gendered generation claim or extend the animal taxonomy beyond the cited statement.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  - φυτεύουσιν
english_gloss: mortal animal, fire, breath, necessity, being melted, being emptied, aid, kindred nature, they plant
observation: "Plants are introduced as divine aid for mortal bodily maintenance: because the mortal animal must live in fire and breath and is thereby melted, emptied, and wasted, the gods plant another living thing from human-kindred nature."
textual_basis: Across 76e-77a, the account turns from nails to the mortal animal's maintenance. It says the mortal animal must live in fire and breath, which melt, empty, and waste it, and that the gods devise help by mixing a kindred nature with other forms and perceptions and planting another animal kind.
limits: This records the teleological introduction of plants as aid for mortal bodily maintenance. It does not include the nail rationale, agriculture, or the later plant soul-capacity account.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "Plants are classified as living things by participation in life, and their soul-capacity is identified with the third soul-kind located between diaphragm and navel: they have no share in belief, calculation, or intellect, but do have pleasant and painful perception with desires."
textual_basis: At 77b, the account says whatever shares in living is most correctly called a living thing, then assigns the present plant kind to the third form of soul. The same span locates that soul-kind between diaphragm and navel and contrasts its lack of belief, calculation, and intellect with its share in pleasant and painful perception and desires.
limits: This records the explicit soul-capacity classification for plants in the cited span. It does not infer a broader botany or compare plants with later animal kinds.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
english_gloss: motion, its own, to calculate, lives, living thing, fixed, rooted, motion by itself
observation: The plant kind is said to live and to be no different from a living thing, but to remain fixed and rooted because it lacks motion from itself and has not been granted the nature needed to use its own motion for calculating or inspecting its own things.
textual_basis: "At 77c, the account denies that generation has given this kind the nature to push away external motion, use its own motion, and calculate or see any of its own things. It then states the consequence: it lives and is a living thing, yet stands fixed and rooted because it is deprived of motion by itself."
limits: This records the stated relation between self-motion, cognition, and rootedness. It does not claim that plants are wholly without perception, since the preceding classification assigns them pleasant and painful perception.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "The body is introduced as an irrigation system for nutrition: the superior makers plant these kinds as food for weaker humans and cut channels through the body so that it may be watered like garden beds by an incoming stream."
textual_basis: At 77c, after the plant classification, the account says the superior beings planted all these kinds as food for weaker humans. It immediately describes the human body as channeled by cuts like garden channels, with the purpose that it be watered from an approaching stream.
limits: This records the nutritional irrigation image and its stated purpose. It does not include the later details of the paired veins.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 77d, the account cuts two hidden spinal veins under the joining of skin and flesh because the body is double, right and left. These veins are sent along the spine, taking the fertile marrow between them, and their purpose is stated as making the marrow flourish and giving the rest an even supply by a downward outpouring.
limits: This records the paired vein placement and watering purpose. It does not include the subsequent crossing of the veins around the head.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: The veins are split around the head and woven across in opposite directions, from right to left and left to right, so that together with the skin they form a bond between head and body where sinews do not encircle the crown.
textual_basis: At 77e, the account says the veins are split around the head, woven through one another in opposite directions, and bent from right to left and from left to right. The stated purpose is that there be a bond for the head toward the body together with the skin, since the head was not wrapped around the crown by sinews.
limits: This records the structural binding function of the crossed veins. It does not include the distinct sensory-distribution purpose also stated in the same span.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 78a-78b, the account explicitly asks for prior agreement on the rule about smaller and larger parts, identifies fire as the smallest-parted kind, and applies the same point to the human belly. limits: This records the stated material premise for the following mechanism. It does not include the woven net construction except as the next use of the premise.
```

```yaml
  - σχοίνους
  - πλοκάνου
english_gloss: belly, veins, watering, web, air, fire, fish-traps, weaving together, funnels, two-footed, cords, plaited thing
observation: "The god uses the containment premise to construct a watering device from belly to veins: he weaves a web of air and fire like fish-traps, with double funnel openings, one of them woven again as two-footed, and with cord-like extensions running through the whole net."
textual_basis: "At 78b-78c, the account says god made use of the finer breath and fire for the watering from the belly to the veins. It describes the device with weaving and fish-trap vocabulary: a web from air and fire, double funnels at the entrance, one funnel made two-footed, and cord-like strands stretched from the funnels to the extremities of the net."
limits: This records the crafted model and materials of the apparatus. It does not yet describe the placement of the funnels in mouth, lung, belly, and nose.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 78c-78d, the account specifies the net's composition and placement. The inner part of the plait is from fire, the funnels and vessel are air-like, one funnel goes into the mouth and is divided through the arteries toward the lung and beside the arteries toward the belly, while the other is split along the channels of the nose to replenish all streams when the mouth route is absent.
limits: This records the anatomical routing of the device. It does not include the subsequent naming of respiration and expiration after 78d.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 78d, after the flow-replenishment clause, the account says the other vessel of the trap is grown around the hollow part of the body. It then describes the air-like apparatus flowing into the funnels, the funnels flowing back, the web passing inward and outward through the porous body, and the bound rays of inner fire following as the air goes each way.
limits: This records the alternating air-fire movement within the cited span. It does not include the following continuation where the process is named and its bodily result is summarized.
```

```yaml
observation: Respiration is presented as a life-sustaining process that waters, cools, nourishes, and keeps the mortal animal alive; the internal fire follows the breath, dissolves food and drink in the belly, divides them finely, and pumps them into the veins like water from a spring into channels.
textual_basis: At 78e-79a, Timaeus names the process in-breathing and out-breathing and says the whole work and affection occurs so that the body, being watered and cooled, is nourished and lives. He then describes the fire inside following the movement of breathing, entering through the belly, taking foods and drinks, melting them, dividing them into small parts, and drawing them into the veins like from a spring into channels so the streams of the veins flow through the body.
limits: This records the stated physiological work of respiration and internal fire. It does not include the following no-void explanation of the breath cycle.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0304

  - σαρκῶν
  - στόματος
  - μυκτήρων
english_gloss: void, breath, pushes, neighboring, filling up, wheel, chest, lung, flesh, mouth, nostrils
observation: "The breathing mechanism is grounded in denial of void: outgoing breath does not enter emptiness, but pushes adjacent air, which in turn pushes the next, so that surrounding air fills the vacated place and the cycle turns like a wheel through chest, lung, body, mouth, and nostrils."
textual_basis: At 79b-79c, Timaeus says there is no void into which any moving thing could enter. Because breath goes out from us, it does not enter a void but pushes what is next from its seat; the pushed item always drives out its neighbor, and everything driven around enters the place from which the breath left and fills it, following the breath like a rotating wheel. The chest and lung release breath outward and are filled again by air entering through porous flesh, while air turning outward through the body pushes inhalation inward through mouth and nostrils.
limits: This records the no-void replacement mechanism. It does not yet give the initiating cause in internal heat.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  - ἐκπνοὴν
english_gloss: blood, veins, hottest, spring, fire, basket-work, air, mouth, nostrils, circle, in-breathing, out-breathing
observation: "The initiating cause of respiration is internal heat around blood and veins: because the hottest part naturally moves toward kindred fire outside, the two exits through the body and through mouth and nostrils alternately push one another, producing the oscillating circle of in-breathing and out-breathing."
textual_basis: At 79d-79e, every animal is said to have its hottest part around blood and veins, like an internal spring of fire. The middle is compared to a woven basket of fire and the outside to air. Since the hot naturally moves outward toward its kin, and there are two exits, one through the body and another through the mouth and nostrils, movement toward one side pushes the other side; as heat changes and the warmer side turns again, the same reciprocal process makes a circle rocked both ways and produces respiration and expiration.
limits: This records the thermal cause assigned to the breathing cycle. It does not claim the mechanism is empirically adequate by modern standards.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "Sound concord and discord are explained by motion similarity: fast and slow, high and low sounds are discordant when their motions in us are unlike, but become concordant when later slower motions overtake earlier faster motions as they become similar."
textual_basis: At 80a-80b, the passage describes sounds as fast and slow, high and low. It marks discord by dissimilarity in the movements they produce in us, then explains concord by the slower later motions catching up with the faster earlier motions as those faster motions are ceasing and becoming similar.
limits: This records the acoustic mechanism of concord and discord only. It does not include the earlier examples in the same span or the distinct affective contrast between unwise pleasure and wise good cheer.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 80c, Timaeus lists thunderbolt falls and the admired cases of attraction involving amber and Heraclean stones. He says there is no pulling for them, and instead appeals to no void, mutual pushing, separation and combination, and changes of place toward proper seats.
```

```yaml
observation: "The respiration account is folded back into nutrition: fire cuts food while moving with breath, fills veins from the belly by drawing up the cut material, and produces nourishment streams throughout the body; the red color of the bodily stream called blood comes from fire's cutting and wiping in moisture."
textual_basis: "At 80d-80e, Timaeus returns to respiration and says it works by the same causes: fire cuts foods, moves inside with breath, fills the veins from the belly by drawing up the cut pieces, and therefore makes streams of nourishment flow through the whole body in living things. The newly cut food from fruits and grasses has many colors through mixture, but red chiefly runs through it, and the nature formed by fire's cutting and wiping in moisture gives the stream through the body the appearance called blood."
limits: This records the connection among respiration, nourishment, and blood color. It does not include the subsequent general filling and emptying rule.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "Bodily filling and emptying follow a cosmic like-to-like rule: blooded parts refill what is emptied by moving toward kindred parts, and the animal declines when more departs than flows in but grows when less departs than flows in."
textual_basis: At 81a-81b, the mode of filling and departure is said to be like motion in the whole, where everything kindred moves toward itself. Blooded things divided within each animal imitate that motion and refill what has been emptied, and the span concludes with the decline and growth rule.
limits: This records the filling, emptying, decline, and growth mechanism only. It does not include the youth-triangle and old-body explanations that follow.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

english_gloss: new, constitution, triangles, stocks, marrow, milk, foods, drinks, cutting, nourishing
observation: "Youth is explained by the strength and freshness of bodily triangles: the new animal's triangles are still firmly locked, soft from recent marrow-formation and milk nourishment, and strong enough to cut the older weaker triangles entering from food and drink, thereby making the animal large through nourishment from many similar parts."
textual_basis: At 81b-81c, the new constitution of the whole animal is said to have new triangles, as if still from the stocks of the kinds, with a strong closure to one another while the whole mass is soft because it has recently come from marrow and been nourished in milk. The triangles entering from outside through foods and drinks are older and weaker than its own, so the new triangles master them by cutting them and nourish the animal from many similar parts.
limits: This records the youth-and-growth side of the triangle account. It does not include the aging reversal that follows.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0311
source_work: Timaeus

  - φθίνει
  - μυελὸν
  - δεσμοὶ
english_gloss: root, triangles, loosens, contests, nourishment, cut, declines, old age, marrow, bonds
observation: "Aging is the reversal of youthful mastery: after many contests over time, the root of the body's triangles loosens, the body can no longer cut incoming nourishment into its own likeness, it is easily divided by what enters from outside, and this defeat is named old age."
textual_basis: At 81c-81d, when the root of the triangles slackens because it has fought many contests against many things over much time, the animal can no longer cut the incoming nourishment into likeness with itself. Instead, it is easily divided by the incoming external things; the whole animal declines when mastered in this way, and the affection is called old age.
limits: This records the aging mechanism up to the naming of old age. It does not yet describe the final release of soul-bonds.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0312
source_work: Timaeus

  - δεσμοὶ
  - ψυχῆς
  - φύσιν
  - ἡδονῆς
  - θάνατος
  - νόσους
  - τραυμάτων
english_gloss: marrow, triangles, bonds, soul, released, nature, pleasure, death, diseases, wounds
observation: "Death is divided by mode of release: when the marrow-triangle bonds can no longer endure and release the soul-bonds, the soul flies away naturally with pleasure; death from diseases or wounds is painful and violent, while death with old age is the least painful and occurs more with pleasure than grief."
textual_basis: At 81d-81e, when the bonds of the triangles around the marrow no longer withstand the strain and come apart, they release the bonds of the soul, and the soul, released according to nature, flies away with pleasure. The explanation adds that what occurs contrary to nature is painful and what occurs as it is naturally disposed is pleasant. Death from diseases and wounds is therefore painful and violent, but death reaching its end with old age according to nature is the least painful and more with pleasure than pain.
limits: This records the contrast between natural and violent death. It does not make a broader ethical claim about choosing death.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "Disease is introduced as elemental disorder: because the body is compacted from earth, fire, water, and air, unnatural excess or deficiency, transfer from proper to alien place, and each kind taking on what does not belong to it produce factions and diseases."
textual_basis: At 82a, Timaeus says the origin of diseases is clear because the body is compounded from four kinds, earth, fire, water, and air. Unnatural excess and deficiency of these, change of place from proper to alien, and each kind of fire and the other elements taking on what is not fitting to itself are said to provide factions and diseases.
limits: This records the first general cause of diseases in the span. It does not include the continuation beyond 82a on hot, cold, dry, and wet reversals.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
english_gloss: same with same, according to proportion, safe, healthy, miss the measure, diseases, destructions
observation: "Health is stated as a rule of proportional exchange: the same thing added to and removed from the same thing in the same way and according to proportion lets the body remain itself, safe, and healthy, while a failure in incoming or outgoing change produces many alterations, diseases, and destructions."
textual_basis: At 82b, after listing reversals among hot, cold, dry, wet, light, and heavy states, Timaeus says that only same-to-same addition and removal in the same way and by proportion preserves the body as sound and healthy. He then says any departure in what comes in or goes out will provide varied alterations, diseases, and destructions.
limits: This records the general proportional-exchange rule for health and disease. It does not identify any single organ or humor as the cause.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "A second class of diseases is introduced from secondary bodily compounds: marrow, bone, flesh, sinew, and blood are named as formations from the primary elements, and the greatest diseases occur when their generation proceeds in reverse, so that these compounds are destroyed."
textual_basis: At 82c-82d, Timaeus shifts to a second consideration of diseases after the primary elemental account. He lists marrow, bone, flesh, sinew, and blood as compounds and says that the greatest diseases become severe when their generation travels backward; the passage then begins the natural order from blood into flesh and sinew.
limits: This records the reverse-generation disease mechanism. It does not claim that every named tissue has a separate disease in this span.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "The healthy order of tissue formation includes two nutritive flows: a viscous oily product from sinews and flesh glues flesh to bone and grows the bone around marrow, while the purest smooth oily triangular kind filters through bone and waters the marrow."
textual_basis: At 82d-82e, after explaining flesh and sinew from blood, Timaeus describes what departs from sinews and flesh as viscous and oily, joining flesh to bone and nourishing the bone around marrow. He then says the purest, smoothest, oiliest kind of triangles passes through the density of the bones, drips from them, and waters the marrow. Health follows when things occur in this order.
limits: This records the stated healthy sequence for tissue, bone, and marrow nourishment. It does not include the pathological reversal that follows.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
  end_marker: 83a
  start_char: 127947
  end_char: 128794
  text_sha256: 34914b24b0a8da26c6dafb6c30179e15584a40733f141fa878d512aa13fd2f3c
greek_terms:
  - τηκομένη
  - σὰρξ
  - φλέβας
  - πνεύματος
  - αἷμα
  - χολὰς
  - ἰχῶρας
  - φλέγματα
  - τροφὴν
  - πολέμια
english_gloss: melting, flesh, veins, breath, blood, biles, ichors, phlegms, nourishment, hostile
observation: "Disease is described as a reversal in which melting flesh enters the veins and changes the blood: mixed with breath, the blood becomes varied by colors, bitterness, sharpness, and saltiness, produces biles, ichors, and phlegms, no longer nourishes the body, loses the order of natural circuits, and becomes hostile to the settled parts."
textual_basis: At 82e-83a, Timaeus says diseases arise when flesh melts and sends its melt backward into the veins. The blood in the veins is then made manifold with breath and with colors and powers, and it holds many biles, ichors, and phlegms. Because these products have been reversed and corrupted, they first destroy the blood, provide no nourishment, move everywhere through the veins without the natural order, and act as enemies to themselves and to the stable body.
limits: This records the pathological blood-and-humor sequence. It does not identify the later color-specific varieties as separate diseases.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "The passage sorts bile-like products by the condition and color of melted flesh: old melted flesh becomes black and bitter, the black can become sharp when bitterness is thinned, bitterness dyed with blood becomes redder, mixture with black becomes greenish, and newly melted flesh under flame adds a yellow color."
textual_basis: At 83a-83b, Timaeus follows the general corruption account with color and taste variations. The oldest melted flesh is hard to digest, blackened by old burning, and bitter; further changes are then tied to sharpness, blood-dyed redness, black-mixed greenness, and the yellow color that appears when new flesh is melted by the fire around flame.
limits: This records the color-and-quality taxonomy in the cited span. It does not treat the later common naming of all these products as bile.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 83b-83c, after listing the color variations, Timaeus says that a common name for all these has been given as bile, either by physicians or by one capable of seeing one genus present in many unlike things. He then distinguishes the other things said to be species of bile by the account each has according to color.
```

```yaml
observation: "Ichor and phlegm are classified by source material and mixture: one ichor is mild blood-serum, another is a savage serum of black sharp bile mixed through heat with salty power and called sharp phlegm, while melting new soft flesh mixed with air and moisture forms bubbles and foam whose white appearance gives the name white phlegm."
textual_basis: At 83c-83d, Timaeus distinguishes a mild ichor from blood-serum and a harsher one from black sharp bile when it is mixed by heat with salty power, calling that product sharp phlegm. He then describes new soft flesh melting with air, being enclosed by moisture, forming bubbles and foam, and producing a visible white color; this whole melt of soft flesh interwoven with breath is called white phlegm.
limits: This records the local classification of ichor and phlegm. It does not include the following daily excretions or the broader statement about instruments of diseases after 83d.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "The tissue that binds flesh to bones is given a disease pathway: when bad regimen makes it rough, salty, and dry instead of smooth and adhesive, it separates from the bones, leaves sinews bare and salty, and returns fallen material into the bloodstream."
textual_basis: At 84a-84b, the binding material is described as normally becoming nourishment for bone and a bond from flesh to bone. When diseased by bad regimen, it changes texture, withdraws from the bones under the flesh and sinews, leaves the sinews bare and full of salt, and falls back into the blood-flow.
limits: This observation records the stated connective-tissue mechanism. It does not claim a modern anatomical equivalence for the tissue or for the named sinews.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "Bone disease is ranked as worse than the preceding tissue disorders, and marrow disease is ranked as the extreme case: bone that cannot breathe through dense flesh heats, mortifies, rejects nourishment, and sends harsher disease material into flesh and blood, while diseased marrow produces the greatest death-directed diseases."
textual_basis: At 84b-84c, the passage calls these bodily affections hard and then says the earlier conditions become still greater when bone lacks sufficient breathing because of dense flesh, heats under mold, mortifies, rejects nourishment, and passes material back through flesh into blood. It then names disease of the marrow's nature from deficiency or excess as the last and greatest condition directed toward death.
limits: This records the local severity sequence from bone to marrow. It does not include the following third class of diseases except as the next transition.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 84c-84d, after the marrow-disease close, Timaeus says a third kind of diseases must be understood as threefold, arising from air, phlegm, and bile. The first case names the lung as steward of the body's airs and describes blocked passages that send no air to some places and more than proper air to others.
limits: This observation records the classification and the opening air example. It does not complete the air-pathology sequence that continues afterward.
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 84e, air arising in separated flesh and unable to go outside is said to produce the same pangs as air that has entered. The greatest pains occur when the air gathers around the sinews and nearby small veins, swells them, stretches the tendons and continuous sinews backward, and gives the diseases their names from the condition's tension.
limits: This records the naming and mechanism in the 84e air case. It does not use the incomplete fever-remedy sentence that continues beyond this span.
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 85a-85b, white phlegm is hard when cut off because of the air in its bubbles, but milder when it has breathings outside the body, where it marks the body with white eruptions and related diseases. The same passage says that, mixed with black bile and scattered over the most divine revolutions in the head, it disturbs them; attacking in sleep is milder, while attacking the waking is harder to escape, and as a disease of sacred nature it is most justly called sacred.
limits: This records the contrast and naming in the phlegm account. It does not identify the disease with any later diagnostic category.
review_status: accepted
```

It then contrasts bile receiving outward breathing, which sends up varied tumors as it boils, with bile confined inside, which makes many fiery diseases; the greatest case is introduced when bile mixes with clean blood and removes the fiber kind from its own differentiated order.

```yaml
observation_id: obs_timaeus_0329

  - τρόμον
  - μυελοῦ
  - ψυχῆς
english_gloss: fibers, blood, bile, old blood, hot, wet, chill, trembling, marrow, soul
observation: "Bile is explained as old blood melted back from the flesh into the blood: when it enters gradually, the fibers congeal and forcibly extinguish it, producing internal chill and trembling; when it flows in greater quantity, its heat overcomes and disorders the fibers and can reach the marrow, burn through, and release the soul-bonds."
textual_basis: At 85d-85e, fibers in blood are said to preserve the natural timing of blood's composition. Bile, naturally old blood that has melted back from the flesh, enters hot and wet, first congealing by the power of the fibers and producing chill and trembling when forcibly extinguished. When more bile flows in, it boils and shakes the fibers into disorder, and if it can master them to the end it passes through to the marrow, burns, and loosens the soul's bonds like ship cables.
limits: This records the bile-and-fiber mechanism in the cited span. It does not include the later element-specific fever classification.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "The account classifies bodily diseases by elemental excess: fire produces continuous burnings and fevers, air produces daily fevers, water produces tertian fevers because it is slower than air and fire, and earth produces quartan fevers because it is slowest and is purified over fourfold periods."
textual_basis: At 86a, after naming diarrheas and dysenteries from expelled bile, Timaeus says a body diseased mostly from excess fire produces continuous burnings and fevers. Disease from air produces daily fevers, water produces tertian fevers because water is more sluggish than air and fire, and earth, being fourth and most sluggish, is purified in fourfold time periods and produces quartan fevers that are hard to remove.
limits: This records the element-periodicity classification in the cited span. It does not decide the medical adequacy of the classification.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "Diseases of the soul are explicitly traced through bodily condition: soul disease is called mindlessness and divided into madness and ignorance, while excessive pleasures and pains are ranked as the greatest soul diseases because they prevent correct seeing, hearing, and participation in calculation."
textual_basis: At 86b-86c, Timaeus turns from bodily diseases to diseases of soul through bodily condition. He says one must agree that disease of soul is mindlessness, and that there are two kinds of mindlessness, madness and ignorance. Whatever affection produces either kind should be called disease, and excessive pleasures and pains are set down as the greatest soul diseases, since a person rushing out of season to seize or flee cannot see or hear anything correctly and is least able to share in calculation.
limits: This records the formal classification and the role assigned to excessive pleasure and pain. It does not yet include the specific sexual example that follows.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "Sexual incontinence is treated as a soul disease caused by bodily condition: excessive fluid seed around the marrow produces many pains and pleasures in desires and begettings, making the person maddened for much of life while others wrongly judge him voluntarily bad rather than diseased."
textual_basis: At 86c-86d, the account says that when seed is abundant and flowing around the marrow, like a tree grown more fruitful than measure, the person gains many pains and pleasures in desires and related begettings. He becomes maddened for much of life through the greatest pleasures and pains, holds his soul diseased and mindless because of the body, and is regarded not as diseased but as voluntarily bad. The span adds that sexual lack of discipline is for the most part a disease of the soul because of one bodily kind's watery and moist state through porous bones.
limits: This records the bodily etiology assigned to the sexual example. It does not generalize all action to compulsion beyond the cited explanation.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "The passage states the maxim that no one is bad willingly and gives bodily condition and uneducated nurture as causes: what is blamed as voluntary badness in pleasure-incontinence is said to be wrongly reproached, since badness comes unwillingly through a bad bodily condition and an uneducated mode of nurture."
textual_basis: At 86d-86e, Timaeus says that almost all cases called lack of control over pleasures and reproached as voluntary badness are not rightly reproached. No one is bad willingly; the bad person becomes bad because of some bad condition of the body and an uneducated nurture, and these things are hostile to everyone and come upon the person unwillingly. The span also adds that the soul has much badness through the body in matters of pain in the same way.
limits: This records the causal maxim and its two named factors. It does not remove the later role of education or law outside the cited span.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 86e-87a, Timaeus says acidic and salty phlegms and bitter or bilious humors that wander through the body, fail to receive external breathing, and are enclosed within, mix their vapor with the motion of the soul. Carried toward the three places of soul, they produce more or fewer and greater or lesser soul diseases according to where each strikes, including varieties of irritability, despondency, boldness, cowardice, forgetfulness, and difficulty learning.
limits: This records the humoral mechanism and named soul effects in the cited span. It does not include the continuation beyond the cited span.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "Badness is traced to two highly involuntary sources: badly formed regimes and civic speeches, together with the absence of healing lessons from youth. The account assigns more blame to those who plant and nurture than to those planted and nurtured, while still urging each person to avoid vice through nurture, practices, and lessons as far as possible."
textual_basis: At 87b, Timaeus says bad regimes and speeches in cities, both private and public, and the absence of healing lessons from youth make bad people bad through two most involuntary causes. The planters are to be blamed more than the planted and the nurturers more than the nurtured, yet one must be eager, as far as possible, through nurture, practices, and lessons, to flee vice and choose its opposite.
limits: This records the causal and responsibility structure in the cited span. It does not give the separate account of bodily and mental therapy that follows.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
english_gloss: bodies, thoughts, therapies, good, beautiful, measureless, proportionate, health, diseases, virtues, vices, soul, body
observation: "The therapeutic discussion is grounded in proportion: the good is beautiful, the beautiful is not without measure, and the living thing that is to be good must be proportionate. The largest and most authoritative proportion for health, disease, virtue, and vice is the proportion of soul itself to body itself."
textual_basis: At 87c-87d, Timaeus says it is fitting to give a reciprocal account of the causes by which bodily and mental therapies are preserved, since it is more just to speak about goods than evils. He states that every good is beautiful, the beautiful is not without measure, and the living thing that is to be such must be assumed proportionate. He then says people perceive and reason about small proportions but are unreasoning about the greatest ones, and that no proportion or disproportion is greater for health and diseases, virtues and vices, than that of soul itself to body itself.
limits: This records the proportional principle and its scope. It does not yet spell out the two imbalance cases.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
english_gloss: soul, strong, body, beautiful, disproportionate, most beautiful, most lovable, overlong-legged, measureless, sharing, labors
observation: A mismatch between a strong large soul and a weaker smaller body, or the reverse, is called not beautiful because the whole living thing is disproportionate in the greatest proportions; the opposite condition is described as the most beautiful and lovable sight for one able to see it.
textual_basis: At 87d-87e, Timaeus says people fail to observe that when a strong and wholly large soul rides a weaker and smaller bodily form, or when the opposite pairing is joined, the whole living thing is not beautiful because it is disproportionate in the greatest proportions. Conversely, the living thing with the opposite condition is the most beautiful and lovable of all sights for one able to behold it. The span then compares a body with overlong legs or another measureless excess, which is ugly and produces labors, sprains, falls, and many evils for itself.
limits: This records the aesthetic and health language of proportion. It does not yet give the later regimen for correcting imbalance.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: Across 87e-88a, the passage states that a soul stronger than the body shakes the whole body from within and fills it with diseases. It then names studies, inquiries, teachings, and battles in speeches as occasions that inflame, shake, and produce flows in the body.
```

```yaml
english_gloss: preservation, soul, body, body, soul, balanced, healthy, mathematician, gymnastics, music, philosophy, beautiful, good
observation: "The single preservation for both imbalance cases is balanced motion: the soul should not be moved without the body nor the body without the soul. The intense intellectual worker must give the body motion through gymnastics, while the body cultivator must repay the soul with music and all philosophy if he is to be rightly called both beautiful and good."
textual_basis: "At 88b-88c, Timaeus states one preservation for both cases: neither move the soul without the body nor the body without the soul, so that by defending themselves they become balanced and healthy. The mathematician or anyone working intensely with the mind must give back bodily motion by associating with gymnastics, while the one carefully shaping the body must repay the soul's motions by using music and all philosophy, if he is to be rightly called beautiful and good together."
limits: This records the reciprocal training rule. It does not specify particular exercises or curriculum.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "Bodily care imitates the whole by countering external and internal affections with motion: a resting body is overcome by burning, cooling, drying, and moistening motions, while a body kept moving and shaken resists them."
textual_basis: Across 88d-88e, Timaeus says bodily parts should be cared for by imitating the whole. The body is burned, cooled, dried, and moistened by incoming and surrounding motions; if left at rest it is overcome, but if kept moving and internally shaken it defends against those motions.
limits: This records motion as bodily defense. It does not include the kinship-ordering rule stated later in the same marker.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "Moderate internal shaking is said to arrange wandering bodily affections and parts into order by kinship. The rule is framed by contrast: placing enemy beside enemy generates bodily wars and diseases, while placing friend beside friend produces health."
textual_basis: At 88e, Timaeus says the body should defend its inner and outer motions according to nature, moderately shaking the wandering affections and parts around the body and ordering them by kinship in relation to one another. He adds that, according to the earlier account of the whole, one must not place enemy beside enemy and let wars and diseases be born in the body, but instead place friend beside friend so as to provide health.
limits: This records the kinship-ordering rule for bodily motion in the cited marker. It does not include the following ranking of kinds of motion.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

observation: "Kinds of bodily motion are ranked by their relation to self-motion: motion in oneself by oneself is best because it is most akin to intellectual and cosmic motion, motion by another is worse, and passive partial motion of a resting body is worst. Exercise is consequently ranked first among bodily purifications, with gentle conveyance second."
textual_basis: "At 89a, Timaeus says the best motion is the motion in oneself by oneself, because it is most akin to intellectual motion and the motion of the whole. Motion by another is worse, and the worst is the kind that moves a resting body part by part through external agencies. He then ranks bodily purifications and constitutions: through exercises is best, and through swingings, voyages, and painless conveyances is second."
limits: This records the ranked motion schema in the cited marker. It does not include the pharmaceutical case that begins at the end of the marker and continues afterward.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: Drug-based purification is admitted under strong necessity but otherwise rejected for anyone with sense. Non-dangerous diseases should not be irritated by drugs, because disease-constitutions resemble beings with appointed life spans and premature drug destruction tends to turn small and few diseases into great and many ones.
textual_basis: At 89b-89c, Timaeus says pharmaceutical purification from medicine is useful under strong necessity but should otherwise not be accepted by someone with understanding. He warns against irritating diseases without great dangers, compares disease-constitutions to living beings with appointed spans, and recommends guidance by regimens as far as leisure allows.
limits: This records the caution about pharmaceutical intervention and the stated reason for regimen. It does not include the prior ranking of bodily motions or the following self-pedagogy transition.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 89d, after saying that one should not irritate a troublesome evil by drugging it, Timaeus concludes the account of the common living being and its bodily part, describing how someone would live most according to reason as both educating and being educated by himself. He then says the thing that will educate must be prepared even more and beforehand, as far as possible, to be most beautiful and best for education.
limits: This records the transition from bodily self-regimen to preparation of the educating part. It does not give the detailed account of that part, which follows.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "The three kinds of soul are each assigned their own motions, and their condition depends on exercising those motions: the kind that spends time in idleness and rest becomes weakest, while the kind in exercise becomes strongest."
textual_basis: At 89e, Timaeus recalls the earlier repeated claim that three kinds of soul have been settled in three places in us and that each has motions. He then states briefly that the one passing time in idleness and keeping its own motions at rest must become weakest, while the one in exercises becomes strongest.
limits: This records the general rule about the soul kinds and their motions. It does not yet identify the ruling kind as a divine guardian, which is stated next.
```

```yaml
observation: The ruling kind of soul is described as a guardian spirit given by god to each person. Because it dwells at the top of the body and lifts us from earth toward heavenly kinship, the human being is called a heavenly rather than earthly plant, with the head as root.
textual_basis: At 90a, after saying that the motions of the soul kinds must be kept proportionate to one another, Timaeus says one should think of the most authoritative kind of soul as a guardian spirit god has given to each person. He identifies it as dwelling at the top of the body and lifting us from earth toward our kinship in heaven, adding that we are rightly called a plant not earthly but heavenly, since the divine suspends our head and root from the place where the first generation of the soul arose.
limits: This records the anthropological image and its placement of the ruling soul. It does not include the contrast between mortal and learning-oriented lives that follows.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 90b, Timaeus says that for the person who has worked hard around appetites or rivalry, all beliefs must have become mortal, and that such a person will lack nothing, as far as possible, in becoming mortal because he has increased that sort of thing. The marker then introduces the contrasting person who has been serious about love of learning and true thoughts and has trained these especially among his own things.
```

```yaml
observation: The discourse declares the assigned account of the whole down to human generation nearly complete and introduces a brief recollection of the other animals so the account remains measured.
textual_basis: At 90e, Timaeus says the initially assigned account of the whole down to human generation seems nearly to have its end, then says the other animals should be recalled briefly so that the discourse may receive a more measured completion.
limits: This records the closure and transition only. It does not include the transformation of unjust men into women or the reproductive mechanism that follows.
```

```yaml
english_gloss: marrow, seed, ensouled, respiration, outflow, desire, generating, desire, disobedient, self-ruling
observation: "Male reproductive desire is explained through marrow called seed: once ensouled and given breath, it produces a life-oriented outflow desire and completes desire to generate; the male genital nature is characterized as disobedient, self-ruling, and not obedient to reason."
textual_basis: At 91b, Timaeus identifies the marrow previously called seed, says it is ensouled and receives breath, and makes in itself a life-desire for the outflow by which it breathes, completing the desire to generate. The same span characterizes the male genital nature as disobedient, self-ruling, and not obedient to reason.
limits: This records the male reproductive mechanism and characterization in the cited span. It does not include the account of womb pathology that follows.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  - πνεύματος
  - νόσους
english_gloss: wombs, uteruses, living thing, desirous, child-making, fruitless, wandering, breath, diseases
observation: The female reproductive organs are described as a desirous living thing oriented toward child-making. When left fruitless beyond the proper time, it is said to become distressed, wander through the body, block breath-passages, prevent breathing, and produce extreme perplexities and varied diseases.
textual_basis: At 91c-91d, Timaeus says the things called wombs and uteruses in women are, for the same reasons, a desirous living thing within them, desiring child-making. When fruitless for a long time beyond the right season, this living thing is distressed, wanders everywhere through the body, blocks the passages of breath, does not allow breathing, casts the person into the utmost perplexities, and causes many other diseases.
limits: This records the textual anatomy and pathology assigned to the womb at 91c-91d. It does not evaluate the medical claim or infer a broader theory of gender.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "Generation is completed through an agricultural image: eros gathers the sexes, fruit is plucked as from trees, invisible and unformed living things are sown into the womb as into a field, reared within, brought to light, and animal generation is completed."
textual_basis: At 91d, Timaeus says that desire and eros bring the sexes together, pluck fruit as from trees, sow invisible and unformed living things into the womb as into a field, rear them inside, bring them to light, and complete the generation of living beings.
limits: This records the field-sowing image and female-generation closure only. It does not include the bird transformation or terrestrial animal account.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "Terrestrial animal body-types are assigned to those who do not use philosophy or heavenly observation: heads and front limbs are drawn toward earth as head-circuits are compressed by idleness, producing four-footed, many-footed, and footless crawling kinds."
textual_basis: Across 91e-92a, Timaeus moves from birds to the terrestrial and beast-like kind. The span connects this class with lack of philosophy and lack of heavenly observation, then explains head and limb orientation, compression of circuits by idleness, and the resulting four-footed, many-footed, and footless crawling forms.
limits: This records the terrestrial transformation taxonomy only. It does not include the bird class before the span or the water-dwelling class afterward.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
  - παράλλαξις
english_gloss: The Phaethon story is explicitly given mythic form but assigned a natural-philosophical truth about celestial deviation.
observation: At 22c-22d the Egyptian priest says the Phaethon story is told in the shape of a myth, but that its truth is the periodic deviation of things moving in the heavens around the earth, which causes destruction by fire over long intervals.
textual_basis: The priest first narrates the Phaethon story (son of Helios burning the earth) and then explicitly separates its mythic shape from its true content, which is celestial deviation and fiery destruction on earth (22c-22d).
limits: The observation records the text's own distinction between mythic form and truth content; it does not assess whether Plato endorses the naturalistic explanation.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  - γεγραμμένα
  - σεσωσμένα
  - ἀγραμμάτους
english_gloss: The priest claims Egyptian temple archives preserve ancient records of all great deeds, while Greek records are newly made and periodically lost.
observation: At 22e-23a the priest asserts that whatever noble or great deeds occurred anywhere are written from ancient times in Egyptian temples and preserved, whereas Greek and other cities' writings are always newly constructed and lost when periodic heavenly floods arrive, leaving their populations illiterate.
textual_basis: The priest contrasts the age and durability of Egyptian temple records with the recency and vulnerability of Greek written records, attributing the difference to cyclical destructions that wipe out writing and literacy among Greeks (22e-23a).
limits: The observation records the priest's in-text claim about archival superiority; it does not assess the historical accuracy of the claim or Plato's endorsement of it.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
  - Νεῖλος
  - σωτὴρ
english_gloss: The Nile's geography is said to spare Egypt from both fire- and flood-based cyclical destructions, establishing why Egypt preserves ancient knowledge.
observation: "At 22c-22e the priest explains that Egypt is spared from both major destruction types: the Nile saves from fire-caused drought at 22d, and the land's unique hydrology prevents flood damage at 22e, while mountain-dwelling herdsmen survive elsewhere."
textual_basis: The priest identifies two destruction modes (fire and water) and in each case explains why Egypt is preserved—the Nile as savior from drought (22d) and the land's upward-flowing hydrology preventing flood damage (22e)—thereby establishing the geographic basis for Egyptian archival priority.
limits: The observation records the geographic reasoning given in the text; it does not evaluate whether the hydrological claims are accurate or whether the setup is dramatic scaffolding for the Atlantis narrative that follows.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  - διπλάσια
  - τριπλάσια
  - ἡμιολίων
  - ἐπιτρίτων
  - ἐπογδόων
english_gloss: The Demiurge cuts harmonic intervals from the cosmic mixture and fills gaps with specific numerical ratios.
observation: At 36a-36b the Demiurge performs a series of cutting and measuring operations on the cosmic mixture, dividing it into double and triple intervals, placing two means in each interval, and filling epogdoic gaps with epitrit intervals, leaving a remainder expressed as a specific numerical ratio.
textual_basis: The text narrates the Demiurge cutting off portions (36a), placing means between extremes, and then at 36b noting that epitrit intervals filled the epogdoic gaps leaving a remainder with the ratio 256 to 243 stated explicitly. The vocabulary of cutting, placing, and filling is craft language applied to the soul-stuff.
limits: The observation records the craft vocabulary and numerical specification but does not interpret the harmonic theory or claim any cosmological meaning beyond what the text states.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: At 36b-36c the Demiurge takes the entire composition, splits it lengthwise into two strips, bends each strip middle-to-middle like a chi into a circle, and joins the ends to themselves and to each other at the point of contact, producing two circles.
textual_basis: The text uses verbs of splitting, bending, joining, and wrapping at 36b-36c, describing physical craft manipulation of the material into a circular form. The comparison to bending like a chi and the joining of ends are explicit in the Greek.
limits: The observation records the craft operations described but does not interpret the cosmological significance of the two circles or the chi-like bending.
review_status: accepted
```
