```yaml
observation: At 36c the Demiurge assigns the outer circle's motion to the nature of the Same and the inner circle's motion to the nature of the Other, then moves the Same rightward and the Other leftward along the diameter, giving primacy to the Same's motion.
textual_basis: The text at 36c states that the outer motion was assigned to the nature of the Same and the inner to the nature of the Other, then specifies directional movement (rightward for Same, leftward for Other) and notes that primacy was given to the Same's motion. The Same and Other are used as category labels for distinct cosmic motions.
limits: The observation records the assignment of Same and Other to motions but does not interpret these as Forms or claim identity with the Eleatic Same and Other.
```

```yaml
observation: At 43d-44a, incoming bodily motions are said to shake the soul's periods, restrain the circuit of the Same, disturb the circuit of the Different, and make the circles move in disorderly directions.
textual_basis: The passage describes violent motion from the bodily channel acting on the soul's revolutions, then lists damage to the intervals, means, bonds, and circular paths before saying that the motions barely hold together and move without rational order at 43e.
limits: This records the narrated mechanism of disorder in the embodied soul; it does not infer a general psychology beyond the cited Timaeus passage.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
limits: This observation records only the exact source attribution; it does not infer endorsement, truth, absence, counterevidence, or a general doctrine.
```

```yaml
textual_basis: The exact cited Greek interval explicitly supplies this attributed proposition.
limits: This observation records only the exact source attribution; it does not infer endorsement, truth, absence, counterevidence, or a general doctrine.
```

```yaml
limits: This observation records only the exact source attribution; it does not infer endorsement, truth, absence, counterevidence, or a general doctrine.
```

```yaml
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The cited passage explicitly states: When earth encounters fire it is dissolved and carried about, but its parts can recombine to become earth again; earth never changes into another kind. Water divided by fire or air can recombine as one fire-body and two air-bodies; and air segments from one dissolved part become two fire-bodies. Conversely, when a small amount of fire is surrounded and overpowered, two fire-bodies combine into one air-form, and when air is mastered and fragmented, from two whole and one half parts one whole water-form is compacted."
textual_basis: The exact cited Greek interval explicitly supplies this attributed proposition.
limits: This observation records only the exact source attribution; it does not infer endorsement, truth, absence, counterevidence, or a general doctrine.
supports_claim_ids:
```

```yaml
  []
```

```yaml
observation: "The cited passage explicitly states: Water mixed with fire, when separated from fire and isolated from air, becomes more uniform and is compressed by the departing elements into solidified forms: hail above the earth, ice on the earth, and the less solidified forms of snow above and hoar-frost on the earth, the latter congealed from dew."
textual_basis: The exact cited Greek interval explicitly supplies this attributed proposition.
limits: This observation records only the exact source attribution; it does not infer endorsement, truth, absence, counterevidence, or a general doctrine.
```

```yaml
textual_basis: The exact cited Greek interval explicitly supplies this attributed proposition.
limits: This observation records only the exact source attribution; it does not infer endorsement, truth, absence, counterevidence, or a general doctrine.
```

```yaml
textual_basis: The exact cited Greek interval explicitly supplies this attributed proposition.
limits: This observation records only the exact source attribution; it does not infer endorsement, truth, absence, counterevidence, or a general doctrine.
```

```yaml
textual_basis: The exact cited Greek interval explicitly supplies this attributed proposition.
limits: This observation records only the exact source attribution; it does not infer endorsement, truth, absence, counterevidence, or a general doctrine.
```

```yaml
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The cited passage explicitly states: Sharp and salty phlegm is the source of all catarrhal diseases, which take on manifold names according to the manifold places into which it flows; and all bodily inflammations arise from bile, which when it finds ventilation outward sends up all sorts of boils while boiling, and when confined within produces many feverish internal diseases."
textual_basis: The exact cited Greek interval explicitly supplies this attributed proposition.
limits: This observation records only the exact source attribution; it does not infer endorsement, truth, absence, counterevidence, or a general doctrine.
supports_claim_ids:
  - claim_timaeus_0229

```

```yaml
observation: "The cited passage explicitly states: The greatest disease arises when bile mixed with pure blood disrupts the class of fibers from their proper arrangement; these fibers are dispersed in the blood to maintain due measure of thinness and thickness, preventing the blood from either flowing out as liquid through a porous body or being too dense and sluggish in the veins; bile, being old blood in its nature and melted back from the flesh into the blood, hot and moist, when it enters little by little is first congealed by the power of the fibers, and in being congealed and forcibly quenched produces internal chill and trembling."
textual_basis: The exact cited Greek interval explicitly supplies this attributed proposition.
limits: This observation records only the exact source attribution; it does not infer endorsement, truth, absence, counterevidence, or a general doctrine.
supports_claim_ids:
  - claim_timaeus_0230
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
limits: This observation records only the exact source attribution; it does not infer endorsement, truth, absence, counterevidence, or a general doctrine.
```
